### M10 decisions
- D-300: M10 is built in parallel with M8 at the owner's request, although MILESTONES lists M9 (and through it M8) as its prerequisite. M10 leaves the voice code (`crates/tandem-voice`, the Dictate UI), onboarding and `apps/web` alone; its decisions are numbered from D-300. F-DI-12's mic in "Create with AI" needs M8's Dictate, so the card has no mic until M8 merges rather than one that does nothing; it's listed above as completing after M8. "Create with AI" is tested against a desktop API fixture (D-8); the real `/v1/ai/teammate-draft` route comes with M13.
- D-301: Gateway details F-PL-01 leaves open. (1) The gateway starts at launch, not on the first session, so `run/gateway.json` exists as soon as the app runs. (2) A lease's grants are read when its session starts; a grant change marks the subject's sessions stale, so the next turn starts a fresh session (resuming the engine's conversation) with a new lease. (3) The search and call tools are listed only for a lease with at least one connected granted plugin. (4) Plugin approvals (F-PL-06/07) arrive with M12; until then the gateway runs only `read`-class tools and answers every other class with a tool error saying it can't ask for plugin approvals yet. No real vendor module ships before M12, so nothing a user can connect is affected. (5) Settings › Plugins and connecting arrive with M12; `plugins_catalog`, `grants_list` and `grants_set` exist now for the teammate's plugin toggles and nudge, and debug E2E builds connect test vendors with `test_plugin_connect`. (6) Resident memory is reported on Linux (`VmRSS`); macOS and Windows report none until a probe is written for them (the metric is `null`, never a guess).

- D-302: Avatars use F-AG-01's 12-hue palette: `AVATAR_HUES` = 25, 52, … 332 (28° apart, none within 15° of red, as UI_SPEC §1.7 asks), index `fnv1a32(id) % 12` unless the user picked one (`teammates.avatar_hue`, 0–11). UI_SPEC §1.7's continuous `fnv1a32 % 360` hue left nothing for a "pick a hue" control to choose from. The gallery's three Avatar snapshots changed with it (hues only).
- D-303: Teammate record details F-AG-01 leaves open. (1) The Lead is made the first time teammates are listed (not at launch), so engine detection has run and the Lead gets an installed chat engine; a profile that ever had a teammate row never gets a new Lead. (2) The hero tile is the pinned teammate, and pinning is exclusive ("Pin as Lead", UI_SPEC UI-70). (3) A deleted teammate keeps its row (its chats name it "Deleted teammate") and frees its name. (4) The Free limit counts teammates besides the auto-made Lead (`is_lead`), deleted ones excluded. (5) A teammate chat is created by `thread_create` with kind `teammate_chat`; a terminal-only teammate's chat is refused with "Only chat engines carry memory and skills." (EngineUnsupported).
- D-304: Briefing details F-AG-06 leaves open. (1) The date is the machine's local date. (2) Defanged lines keep their text with `‹ ›` in place of `< >`, so the teammate can still read its own note. (3) A memory file over its cap is cut at the cap in the briefing with "[Memory over its budget: the rest was left out.]"; writes through `tandem__memory_write` can't go over. (4) The briefing file is per chat and rewritten whenever the chat starts a new engine session (so memory edits and new skills reach the next session), and removed with the chat. (5) ACP agents take no system prompt, so the briefing opens the first prompt of every new agent session, not every turn. (6) Skills the user made, added from the library or opened are "seen" (the `skills_seen` setting); any other skill folder is the teammate's own and "New", and while "Allow teammates to write skills" is off it's listed with that reason and left out of the index.
- D-305: Teammate settings and creation details. (1) F-AG-05's "May create routines" toggle is absent until routines exist (M11 builds them and the schedule tool; MILESTONES §2 rule 4: unbuilt controls are absent, not disabled). (2) The memory caps are PRODUCT_SPEC's 2k / 4k / 8k / 16k characters with ABOUT_YOU.md at 2k; UI_SPEC UI-76's 3,000 / 6,000 / 12,000 / 24,000 and 1,500 disagree with the feature spec and ARCHITECTURE, which win. (3) UI_SPEC's per-place access select and "Who may I message" list have no backing in PRODUCT_SPEC; places are folders the teammate may work in, and mail goes to any teammate that may receive it. (4) The "Default engine for new teammates" setting isn't added: the sheet starts on Claude Code when it's installed, else the first installed chat engine, the same default as Thread mode. (5) "Learn after each turn" in Settings › Teammates turns learning off for every teammate when off; on, each teammate's own Learn mode decides. (6) Create with AI needs Pro or Team, a sign-in and a connection (F-AG-04); the request carries the description, the installed chat engines and connected plugin ids; the answer is checked before it fills anything.
- D-306: Learn details F-AG-08 leaves open. (1) Only completed turns learn (not cancelled or failed ones). (2) Ask mode's pending suggestion sets live in the running app; a restart drops undecided ones rather than keeping model output on disk. (3) A suggestion set Auto can't write (over the cap) is dropped and logged by code, never applied in part. (4) Engines without a headless mode (ACP agents) don't learn; the follow-up uses the engine's default model when the registry has no `cheap_model`.
- D-307: Mail details F-AG-12 leaves open. (1) Teammates have no pause of their own in the data model, so the "paused" refusal never arises; the global pause queues mail rather than refusing it (AC2). (2) Mail held by the rate limit goes out on the next delivery pass once the hour allows it (a new mail, Retry, resuming, or launch); nothing polls. (3) An idle chat in the same thread of work takes mail only at Read or Edits access; a Full-access chat never runs mail, so the recipient wakes in a fresh chat instead. (4) A woken chat carries the sender's thread of work, so a reply comes back to the sender's chat when that chat is idle. (5) Retry applies to failed mail and mail held by the rate limit; refused mail stays refused. (6) `@name` in a teammate's composer is mail from that teammate (UI-71), so its "May message teammates" applies; `mail_send` without a sender is mail from "You". A teammate can't mail itself. (7) The engine receives "From {sender}:\n{body}" as the turn's message; the transcript shows the mail row in its place. (8) UI-71's "Who may I message" list is left out (D-305 (3)).

### M10 log
- M10 task 1 (gateway): built-ins registry, vendor modules and test vendors, grants on leases, search/call listing, the watchdog and metrics, `GatewayService`, the plugins repo and commands, E2E-M10-8. Found on the way: the gateway was started lazily by the first thread session, so `run/gateway.json` (MILESTONES' verification reads it) would never exist before a thread ran; it now starts at launch.
- M10 task 2 (teammate record): core rules, the teammates repo, teammate chats in the threads repo, `TeammatesService`, commands and `teammates:changed`, the avatar palette. The first gate run of task 1 failed only on the API tests: `with-infra.sh` had started Postgres but it wasn't accepting connections yet; a rerun with Docker already up passed.
- M10 task 3 (briefing): `briefing.rs`, delivery in the three adapters, the memory and skills stores, the starter skills, chat spawns in the spawn log, E2E-M10-2's briefing tests. Found on the way: the memory store's first `write` took its cap from the caller, so a caller passing the notes cap could write a 16k ABOUT_YOU.md; its test caught it, and `write` now picks each file's own cap.
- M10 task 4 (memory): the write checks, the change log and Undo, `tandem__memory_write` and `tandem__read_skill` on the gateway, the memory commands, `MemoryTab` and the byte-exact notes parser.
- M10 task 5 (skills): the skills commands, "New" until seen, the Teammates settings page with "Allow teammates to write skills", `SkillsTab` with its editor and library.
- M10 task 6 (session search): the index on turn end, the scoped search and its built-in, E2E-M10-4. Its first run hit the Free plan's teammate limit in the spec (a second teammate besides the Lead); the spec now uses the Lead as the other teammate.

## M11 checklist (MILESTONES M11)
Built on branch `m11-tasks-routines` (from `m10-teammates-gateway`, since M11 needs M10), in parallel with M10's remaining tasks, M8, M12, M13, M14 and M17. Owns F-TK-01…04 and F-RT-01…04; completes F-SH-10 (the Tasks tab), F-SH-13 (the tray's "Pause routines"), F-DB-02 ("Ready for review" and task-review items), F-BL-04 (routines pause on lapse) and F-AG-05 (the Routines link). Then F-RT-05 from M16 on the same branch. This section holds M11's state, decisions (D-900 to D-949) and log.
- [x] 1. Task store (F-TK-01). `tandem_tasks::TaskStore` keeps the tracker in `$DATA/tasks/`: `meta.json` (next number, projects, labels, the six statuses, actors with the human `me`, the `local` team), `issues/ISSUE-<n>.json` (id `TAN-<n>`, a revision, title, Markdown description, status, priority 0–4, labels, project, assignee, timestamps, archived, result, attempts), and per issue an append-only `ISSUE-<n>.history.jsonl` (one line per changed field: time, actor, MCP connection, from, to) and `ISSUE-<n>.comments.jsonl` (threaded by `parent_id`). Every write takes an exclusive lock file under `locks/` (per issue, `meta`, `idempotency`, `connections`; std `File::try_lock`, 5 s, then `UNAVAILABLE`), so the app and any number of MCP server processes can write at once; issue files are replaced atomically (`tandem_core::atomic`). `save_issue` creates (a title is needed) or patches at `expected_revision` (required for a patch; a stale one is `REVISION_CONFLICT` with the current revision), and a patch that changes nothing writes nothing. Idempotency keys (≤128) return the first recorded result for 24 h without writing again, for issues and comments; older entries are compacted when the store opens. Labels, projects and assignees must exist; `null` clears an assignee or project. `connections.json` holds one connection per engine session with the actor its writes are recorded as; a revoked or unknown connection is `CONNECTION_REVOKED`. `list` filters by status, assignee, label, project and text, newest first, with an opaque cursor at a position (updated, number), pages ≤ 50. Rust `tandem-tasks`: store 9, lock 1.
- [x] 2. MCP server (F-TK-02). `tandem-tasks mcp --dir <tasks dir> --connection <id>` is an rmcp stdio server (`transport-io`) that speaks protocol versions 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18 and 2025-03-26 (a client asking for another gets 2025-11-25), with the instructions text ARCHITECTURE §9.1 gives and the 17 tools of §9.3, in that order, each with a draft 2020-12 input schema that allows no other properties (serde refuses unknown fields too). Outputs are `structuredContent` with a text copy; errors are tool results with `isError` and `{code, message}` (`REVISION_CONFLICT` carries `current_revision`). `get_issue` and `list_comments` wrap descriptions and comment bodies as `{untrusted: …}`; `get_issue` adds the last 20 comments. Issues are also resources (`tandem-tasks://issues/TAN-<n>`). Every call first checks the connection: a revoked or unknown one fails the call with JSON-RPC error -32001, `data.code` `CONNECTION_REVOKED`. At most 8 calls run at once, on blocking threads. `--connection-env <VAR>` reads the connection from the environment instead (terminal agents). In the app, `services::tasks::TasksService` opens `$DATA/tasks`, revokes every connection an earlier run left live, and gives each engine session its own connection (D-901): thread and teammate-chat sessions get the server as `tandem_tasks` next to the gateway (Claude's `--mcp-config` file, Codex's `-c mcp_servers.tandem_tasks.*` overrides, ACP's `mcpServers`), and Claude Code and Codex panes get it in their provider home's own config when "Expose tasks to terminal agents" is on (default), with the pane's connection in `TANDEM_TASKS_CONNECTION`; turning the setting off takes the entry out at the next launch. Connections are revoked when the engine exits, the pane closes, the agent hands the pane back, or the chat starts a new session. The binary is found next to the app (`TANDEM_TASKS_BIN` in debug builds), like `tandem-hook` (D-68). Fixture `tests/fixtures/conformance.jsonl` drives the conformance test. Rust `tandem-tasks` f_tk_02: 4 (conformance at each version: 17 tools, every tool called; an unknown version; revocation; 250 issues in 5 pages with new issues added mid-way); `tandem-engines` 2 (Codex overrides, native config for Claude Code and Codex); app `tasks` 3.
- [x] 3. Tasks UI (F-TK-03, UI-80/81). Commands `tasks_meta` (teammates synced as actors first), `tasks_list` (the store's pages), `task_get` (`TaskDetail`: issue, comments, history), `task_save`, `task_comment`, `task_archive`, `task_restore`, `task_connections_list`, `task_connection_revoke` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 first; the UI writes as `me`), and `tasks:changed {ids}` from a `notify` watcher on `issues/`, batched 120 ms, so a write by anyone (the UI, an MCP server, another process) reaches the UI well inside 500 ms (D-905). `stores/tasks.ts` loads the meta and every page of issues and refreshes on the event; `features/tasks`: `TasksPanel` (the sidebar's Tasks tab, which only said "arrives later" before, and Agent mode's Tasks page from the rail's new footer and the `tasks.open` palette command): quick add (Enter, into To do), the list grouped by status with ID, title, labels, priority and the assignee's avatar, row × to archive (toast with Undo), "Show archived" with Restore, empty, loading and error states; wide panels show the open task beside the list, narrow ones instead of it with Back. `TaskDetail`: the title edits inline (Enter or blur saves, Esc drops), status, priority, assignee and project save as they change, labels toggle, the description edits with explicit Save / Cancel (Mod+Enter), the result, threaded comments with Reply, and the history. The merge rule (`merge.ts`): unsaved title or description text is a draft on the issue it started from; a newer copy from another writer updates every other field at once, and when it changed the drafted field too the field is in conflict, "Someone else changed the {field} while you were editing." with Keep mine (saves yours on top) and Take theirs; saves always send the revision the draft is on, and a lost race refetches and asks again. The fake Claude gained `tasks <tool> <json>`, which starts the `tandem_tasks` server from its `--mcp-config` and calls one tool. Desktop vitest tasks 9; app `tasks` +2 (ids from file names; a write is announced within 500 ms); E2E-M11-1 (3 tests: an agent's `save_issue` shows within 500 ms; the agent's status change keeps the user's unsaved title and both are saved; the same-field banner and Keep mine; axe in the dark theme).
- [x] 4. Task execution (F-TK-04, UI-82/83/84). `task_attempts` repo (state queued/setup/running/reviewing/in_review/stopped/failed/done, lane, thread, verdict); `services/tasks_exec.rs` `TaskExecService` behind an `ExecHost` trait (`task_host.rs` makes the lane, starts a task thread or an unattended teammate chat with the `tandem_tasks` server bound, removes lanes): Execute needs sign-in and Pro, at most 4 attempts hold a slot and the rest queue with a position; the briefing fences the issue text with `untrusted::fence` (rule 8 case); the agent's progress comments and result go through its tasks server; a finished run moves the issue to In review with its result, then the optional review runs on a different chat engine and posts "Verdict: Approve" or "Verdict: Request changes" (posted from its reply if it didn't comment); Stop during lane creation sets a cancel flag and rolls the lane back, so nothing is spawned; Resume continues the thread with a note, Retry queues a fresh attempt; attempts left mid-way are stopped at launch; sign-out or a plan change stops every run. Commands `task_execute`, `task_attempts_list`, `task_attempt_stop/resume/retry`, event `tasks:attempt` (ARCHITECTURE §3 first), test hook `test_task_lane_delay`. UI: Execute… on the task (`ExecuteDialog`: workspace, runner (teammate or engine), isolation (new worktree by default), access (Edits default, Full only with a checkbox), review, note; the sign-in / Pro callout with Upgrade) and the Runs list with Stop, Resume, Retry and the verdict. App `tasks_exec` 10 tests; vitest tasks 11; E2E-M11-2 (4 tests: assigning spawns nothing; Execute through the UI makes a lane, progress comments, In review with the result and an Approve verdict; a 5th run queues at position 1 and starts when one finishes; Stop during lane creation leaves no lane, no spawn, no worktree).
- [x] 5. Routine model and schedules (F-RT-01). `tandem_routines::cron::CronExpr` parses standard 5-field cron: `*`, numbers, ranges, steps (`*/n`, `a-b/n`, `a/n`), comma lists, month and day names in any case, day of week 0–7 with 0 and 7 both Sunday (`6-7`, `SAT-SUN` and `5-0` all end on Sunday), and the usual OR when day of month and day of week are both restricted. Errors say what's wrong in words ("Step 61 is larger than the minute range (0–59)", "A cron expression has 5 fields …; this one has 4", "The range 5-1 in the minute field runs backwards"). `schedule::ScheduleSpec` (daily times, weekly days and times, an interval of 15 minutes to a week, or cron, plus an IANA time zone) resolves next runs on the wall clock with `chrono-tz` by the documented rule (D-902): a time that doesn't exist that day runs shifted forward by the gap (2:30 → 3:30), a time that happens twice runs once at its first occurrence; intervals run at multiples of the interval since the epoch, unaffected by DST. A schedule that fires more often than every 15 minutes is refused, as is one that never runs (no run in eight years). `words()` turns a schedule into `ScheduleWords` (every day, weekdays, weekends, named days, every n minutes, hourly at :mm, or the cron itself) for the UI's i18n strings, and `describe_en()` says it in English ("Weekdays at 9:00 AM", "Every day at 7:30 AM, 12:00 PM, and 6:45 PM"). `model::RoutineInput::validate` checks the name (1–80), the instruction (≤ 8,000), the teammate, the schedule, and that Full access comes with `fullConfirmed` (the form's "This routine will act without asking."). Rust `tandem-routines` schedule 10: weekdays and weekends with their next runs, spring-forward in New York, Berlin and Sydney, fall-back, the cron table, every refusal message, DOM-or-DOW, Feb 29 and Feb 30, words, intervals, the model.
- [x] 6. Scheduler (F-RT-02). `tandem_routines::scheduler::decide` is the pure rule: paused (by the user or the plan) waits; no next run gets one from now; due within 24 h fires (late when more than 2 minutes after its slot); due longer ago is logged and moved on; a run still going skips the slot with "previous run still in progress". After a fire the next run counts from the slot or now, whichever is later, so any number of missed slots catch up once. Store repo `routines` (`routines`, `routine_runs`, `error_notice_log`): a slot is claimed with `INSERT OR IGNORE` on `UNIQUE(routine_id, due_at)`, so the GUI and a headless pass can't both run it; runs left `running` by a process that went away settle as errors; history keeps the last 100 runs per routine. `services::routines::RoutinesService` ticks every 30 s of the injected clock (a pass at once at start, for catch-up), saves routines (Pro, a live teammate, `RoutineInput::validate`, a schedule that runs), pauses and resumes (from now, no catch-up for the paused stretch), Run now (refused while a run is going), deletes (chats stay), and pauses everything on a plan lapse and resumes on upgrade (F-BL-04, `paused_by_plan`, from the entitlement events). A run checks the teammate (deleted → "The teammate this routine runs as was deleted.", no spawn), the plan, sign-in and the teammate's chat engine, then `AppRoutineHost` makes a fresh unattended teammate chat at the routine's access titled with its name (`title_source` routine, so auto-title leaves it), sends the instruction and polls the chat until the turn ends: Done is "ok" with the notification "Ready for review — {routine}", anything else an error. Failures set `last_error`/`last_error_kind` on the row, with one notification per error type per routine per day. A routine's chat runs with `Origin::Routine { grants_full }` from the routine's own access (rule 4), not the mail cap other unattended teammate chats get. Commands `routines_list`, `routine_get`, `routine_preview_schedule`, `routine_save`, `routine_delete`, `routine_pause`, `routine_run_now`, `routine_runs` and events `routines:changed`, `routines:run` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 rows updated first). Rust: `tandem-routines` scheduler 3; app routines 9 (fires once at T±30 s with a chat named after the routine; exactly one late run after being closed; a deleted teammate errors with no spawn and one notification; Run now; paused; overlap; Pro, Full and plan lapse; headless runs only background routines; the lock).
- [x] 7. Background runner (F-RT-03). The launchers (first half, above) plus `tandem --run-routines` (`headless::main`): it takes `$DATA/run/app.lock` without waiting (std `File::try_lock`, `app.lock.json` says who holds it) and exits 0 at once when it's held; otherwise it opens the app's services without a window, loads the cached engine report and detects engines, and when the cached plan has background routines runs one pass over the routines whose run mode is background (catch-up included), waits for them (50 min cap) and exits 0; OS notifications go through `notify-rust` (a recording notifier under `TANDEM_E2E`). The GUI's scheduler takes the same lock first (retrying every 5 s while a headless pass has it) and holds it for its life. `routines_background_set` installs or removes the platform launcher at once (Pro; the AppImage path when there is one, else the executable; `TANDEM_HOME` passed on) and `routines_background_status` reports it; saving a background routine installs the launcher when it isn't. `scripts/seed-routine.ts` creates a profile's database with the app's migrations and seeds a teammate and a background routine (`--due-now`). MILESTONES' verification passes (`tandem --run-routines` exits 0; the binary is `tandem`, not `tandem-desktop`). The E2E (E2E-M11-5) comes with the Routines UI.
- [x] 8. Routines UI (F-RT-04, UI-50..53). `features/routines`: `RoutinesPage` (Agent mode's rail footer "Routines" with the active count, the teammate page's header link, the `routines.open` palette command): the header with the background runner chip ("Background runner on" with Turn off, or "Runs only while Vibezeo is open" with Turn on background runs, Pro), the today strip (a 24 h track with done, failed, running and upcoming markers, their tooltips and a now line; hidden under 900 px), a row per routine (status, name, a Full badge, teammate, the schedule in words, Next with the exact time in a tooltip, the last result badge that opens the history, Run now, ⋯ Edit / Pause or Resume / Open last run / Run history / Delete…, and a failure line with its fix: Choose teammate or Run now, and Open run), the row keys (Enter, Space, P, H, Delete, N for new), the empty state with three suggestion chips that pre-fill the editor, loading and error states, the Free upsell and the plan-lapsed banner. `RoutineEditor` (a Sheet with the Esc guard): teammate (or "Routines need a teammate on a chat engine." with New teammate), name, the instruction with the guidance tip and the 20-character minimum, `CronInput`, access with the Full warning and its required checkbox, background runs (Pro), Save and Save and run now (Mod+Enter saves). `CronInput` (UI-52): Daily, Weekly (day toggles, Weekdays / Weekends), Interval and Cron (mono, the field hint and a cheat sheet), the time zone, and the core's preview as you type: the words through i18n (`schedule.ts`, the same rules as `describe_en`), the next 3 runs in the routine's zone and a clock-change note when one falls in the next 60 days; the core's reason replaces the preview when it refuses. `RunHistory` (UI-53): trigger (Scheduled, Run now, Caught up after Vibezeo opened), duration, result, Open chat, the error, All / Failed, older pages and the retention note. `RoutineView.last` gives each row its newest run. F-SH-13's "Pause routines": the `pause_all_routines` setting, toggled from the tray (`tray.rs`: Open Vibezeo, Pause routines, Quit, shown while the background runner is installed), the `routines.pause` palette command and Settings › Teammates; the scheduler fires nothing while it's on and drops the slots that pass. Test hook `test_routines_tick {at}` (ARCHITECTURE §3 first). App tests +4 (pause, `tick_at`, tray); vitest routines 10; E2E-M11-3 (4 tests), E2E-M11-4 (2), E2E-M11-5 (2), E2E-M11-6 (2). The E2E runner puts recording `systemctl` and `crontab` first on the app's PATH and sets `TANDEM_LAUNCHER_ROOT`, and the spec reset deletes routines, turns the background runner off and clears the pause.

The gateway's built-ins gain `tandem__schedule_list`, `_create`, `_update` and `_delete` (`create` takes name, instruction, a 5-field cron, an optional IANA zone and access `read` or `edits`; `update` takes an id and any of those plus `paused`), in a teammate chat's lease only when its "May create routines" is on (`BuiltinSet::for_teammate(may_message, may_schedule)`, now from the teammate's settings in the thread lease and the briefing's tool guidance). `TeammateTools` answers them through `RoutinesService::{made_by, save_for_teammate, delete_for_teammate}`: a routine is the teammate's own (`created_by_teammate_id`, runs as the teammate), Edits unless it asks for Read, Full refused on create and on update, list/update/delete see only its own, and each create or update raises a `routine_changed` notification ("{name} scheduled a routine: {routine}" / "{name} changed the routine {routine}", with the schedule in words). The `tandem-tasks` MCP server ships with the installers: `scripts/build-sidecars.sh <target>` builds it (both arches lipo'd for `universal-apple-darwin`) into `src-tauri/binaries/tandem-tasks-<target>`, and `release-local.sh` and `release.yml` build it and pass `--config src-tauri/tauri.sidecars.conf.json` (`bundle.externalBin`), so Tauri installs it beside the app's executable, where `tasks_bin_from` finds it (`TANDEM_TASKS_BIN` stays the debug override). ### M11 decisions
- D-900: The tracker's records have one shape everywhere: the files under `$DATA/tasks/`, the MCP tools and the app's IPC all use the snake_case fields ARCHITECTURE §9.2 and §9.3 give (`expected_revision`, `next_cursor`, …), rather than camelCase on IPC as the rest of the app does, so the UI and agents read the same records and nothing is translated twice. Their TypeScript names carry a `Task` prefix (`TaskComment`, `TaskLabel`, …) so they never collide with other protocol types. A new issue starts in Todo with priority 0 (none); comments don't change an issue's revision, so an agent's comment never makes the user's open edit stale.

- D-901: How agents get the tasks server (F-TK-02 leaves it open). (1) The server is named `tandem_tasks` everywhere (an underscore, since Codex's config keys are TOML). (2) A connection is one engine session: a thread's or teammate chat's session, or an agent pane's run; its writes are recorded as the teammate (teammate chats), the thread's id (plain threads, shown as an agent) or `me` (terminal agents, which act for the user at the keyboard). (3) Terminal agents get it only through provider homes Vibezeo owns, for the engines whose MCP config it knows how to edit (Claude Code's `.claude.json`, Codex's `config.toml`); other terminal engines don't get it yet. Their config is shared by every pane of the account, so it names an env var (`--connection-env TANDEM_TASKS_CONNECTION`, forwarded with Codex's `env_vars`) and each pane's env carries its own connection; a server started without one has every call refused. (4) Engine processes never outlive the app, so at launch every live connection of an earlier run is revoked.

- D-902: Routine schedule details. (1) The DST rule above is the documented one; it holds for cron, daily and weekly schedules alike, and intervals follow real time. (2) Intervals are at least 15 minutes (PRODUCT_SPEC F-RT-01), not UI_SPEC UI-52's 5, and a cron that fires more often is refused the same way. (3) UI-51's "If missed: Skip it / Run once when Tandem opens" isn't offered: F-RT-02 makes a missed run within 24 h run once, late, always. (4) The form's default access is Edits (F-RT-01); the teammate's "Access for unattended work" setting (M10's page) isn't read by routines, which have their own access.
- D-903: Background launcher details F-RT-03 leaves open. (1) The files go under `TANDEM_LAUNCHER_ROOT` when it's set (tests, E2E), else the user's home. (2) On Linux, when `systemctl --user` fails (no user manager, a container), the unit files are removed again and a crontab line tagged `# tandem-routines` is used; installing replaces any earlier line and uninstalling removes only that line, keeping the user's own. (3) Task Scheduler sets no environment of its own, so with `TANDEM_HOME` the task runs `cmd /c "set "TANDEM_HOME=…" && "<exe>" --run-routines"`. (4) A failed `launchctl bootstrap` removes the plist it wrote, so an entry on disk always means it's registered.

- D-904: Scheduler and run details F-RT-02/03 leave open. (1) A routine's instruction is the user's own words and is sent as the turn's message, not fenced as untrusted; a routine a teammate made with the schedule tool (F-RT-05) is its own instruction to itself, sent the same way and marked "created by {name}". Task bodies, which anyone with the files can write, are fenced (F-TK-04). (2) The GUI runs every routine; a headless pass runs only routines whose run mode is background. (3) A deleted teammate's routine stays, with the error on its row (F-RT-02 AC3), not deleted with the teammate as UI-76's copy says. (4) A run that isn't Done when its turn ends (failed, stopped, interrupted, its chat deleted) is an error; a run waiting for an approval keeps waiting. (5) `--run-routines` without background routines on the cached plan (Free, offline) exits 0 without running anything, and a signed-out profile's runs fail with "Sign in to run routines." (6) Run now is recorded as a manual run (a marker in `routine_runs.error`, since the schema has no column) for UI-53's "Run now" trigger.

- D-905: Tasks UI details F-TK-03 leaves open. (1) The UI hears about changes from an `issues/` file watcher (`tasks:changed`), not the `tasks_subscribe` channel ARCHITECTURE first listed: writes come from other processes (the MCP servers), which only the files see. (2) Tasks are one local tracker for the whole app, not per workspace as UI-80's workspace picker suggests (F-TK-01's `$DATA/tasks/` is one store). (3) The list is grouped by status; UI-80's drag-and-drop board, bulk actions and filter chips aren't built (F-TK-03 asks for the grouped list), so the page offers nothing it doesn't do. (4) Agent mode's rail gets a footer with Tasks (and Routines with task 8); while a footer page is open no teammate row is highlighted, and a selected row's age text is fg-muted, since fg-subtle on the accent tint failed axe's contrast check (found by E2E-M11-1, an M10 row style).
- D-906: Task execution details F-TK-04 leaves open. (1) Access Full is offered with an explicit per-task confirmation (PRODUCT_SPEC F-TK-04 allows it when chosen for that task; UI-82's always-disabled Full is the stricter mock) and the unattended session's origin is `Origin::Task { grants_full }`, so rule 4 holds. (2) The review runs on a chat engine other than the runner's (the first installed one) and, when none differs, on the same engine in a fresh session; when the reviewer gave its verdict only in its reply (a `Verdict:` line at a line start), the app posts that reply as the comment; with no such line the run records no verdict rather than guess one. (3) Stop before the session exists is a cancel flag checked after lane creation, which removes the lane: an abort mid-`git worktree add` could leave half a worktree. (4) Attempts are stopped, not resumed, after a relaunch, and sign-out or a plan change stops all runs (Pro feature). (5) `test_task_lane_delay` (test builds only) lets E2E stop a run during lane creation.

- D-907: Routines UI details F-RT-04 and UI-50..53 leave open. (1) Run now while a run is going is refused by the core with F-RT-02's "previous run still in progress" and the row's toast says so; UI-50's "asks first" would offer a second run the scheduler doesn't make. (2) The editor has no "If missed" choice (F-RT-02 gives every routine one late catch-up within 24 h) and no Plugins picker (unattended runs use the teammate's own grants, M10). (3) The interval builder's minimum is the core's 15 minutes (D-902), not UI-52's 5. (4) Each CronInput tab keeps its own values, so switching tabs never replaces a cron expression and nothing asks "Replace your cron expression?". (5) "Pause routines" (F-SH-13) is one setting, `pause_all_routines`, shared by the tray, the palette and Settings; while it's on nothing fires and passing slots are dropped, not caught up (a pause isn't a missed run). The tray shows while the background runner is installed; Dictate, its other reason, isn't built. (6) E2E moves the scheduler's time with `test_routines_tick {at}`, one pass as if the clock said `at`, instead of starting the app on a fake clock, which would also move sign-in tokens and the API's view of time. E2E-M11-4's "closed and reopened" is the first passes at the reopening time: an E2E relaunch comes back signed out (the E2E keychain lives in memory) and routines need Pro; the relaunch path itself (settle, then the first tick) is the unit test `f_rt_02_2`. (7) The rail's Routines count is the active (not paused) routines.

- D-908: Schedule tool details F-RT-05 leaves open. (1) The tool takes a cron expression, not the builder's kinds: every schedule is one, and the row says it in words. The zone defaults to this computer's. (2) A teammate's routine runs as that teammate; an update that doesn't name an access keeps the routine's, including Full when the user chose it in the editor, and a teammate can only set Read or Edits. (3) "Created by" stays when the user edits the routine (the user's edit doesn't take it over), so the teammate can still list and change it. (4) The notification is its own kind, `routine_changed`, in the Routines group of the notification settings; the routine's schedule in words is its body.

- D-909: The download build and plan-gated controls (follow-up). (1) Where `auth.hosted` is false, every "sign in" or "upgrade" promise in M11's screens uses the Upgrade dialog's early-access sentence and offers no button. (2) Plan-gated controls with nothing to act on are absent, not disabled: `routines.pause` gets `EnabledWhen::Routines` and the Settings row is left out, while Open Routines stays (its page explains Pro). (3) The sidecar is in a separate Tauri config passed only by the release paths, because tauri-build checks every `externalBin` exists at each `cargo build`; dev, the gate and E2E keep using `TANDEM_TASKS_BIN`. Built binaries under `src-tauri/binaries/` are ignored.

### M11 log
- M11 task 1 (task store): `tandem-tasks` model, lock files and `TaskStore`, 10 tests; the concurrency test runs ten rounds of two threads saving at the same revision. Found on the way: the container's `apt-get install zsh` stopped at an interactive conffile prompt; `dpkg --configure -a --force-confold` finished it.

- Merged `product-m0-m5` (0.3.0, M13, invariants) and M10's memory, skills and session search. A first `gen:protocol` after the merge failed on them and left `packages/protocol/src/generated` emptied (the script removes it before generating), so always check its exit status.

The headless mode and the app wiring follow.

Notifications gained `raise_routine` (no window needed: the preferences now come from the store, not `AppState`) and `set_notifier`. Their first run failed five tests for the specs' own reasons: two WebDriver selectors mixed CSS with `=text` (chain `$(css).$("button=…")` instead), which left the editor open and failed the next two tests behind its overlay, and M11-6 read `.code` off the E2E `invoke` error, which is an `Error` whose message holds the JSON. Axe then found the today strip's now line with an `aria-label` and no role, and a `list` whose items sat inside tooltip wrappers; both are `img`s in a `group` now.
