  - The old step ran `git diff "$VERCEL_GIT_PREVIOUS_SHA" HEAD`. Vercel clones only the latest commits, so after a large merge that commit isn't in the clone, and the step failed with "bad object" and exit 128. A local shallow clone shows this. The new step checks that the commit exists first and builds if it doesn't.
  - Feature branches no longer make preview deployments. Their gate checks the site, and with a dozen parallel branches they used up the plan's daily deployment allowance.
  - `apps/web/package.json` pins Node 22 (`engines`), because the demo's Vite 7 build needs Node 20.19+ or 22.12+ and the project setting isn't in the repository.
  - With Vercel's exact install and build commands, all 12 live branches and `product-m0-m5` built locally. The failures came from the deployment setup, not from any branch's code.
- D-810: The browser demo runs early access, as the 0.3.0 download does after "Start using Vibezeo". That's the core's `continue_offline` state: not signed in, offline, not hosted, no email. The entitlement matches what the core builds without a token: Free, source `free`, no credits. Until now the demo was signed in as demo@vibezeo.com. So the title bar showed an avatar email and a red "0 credits" button, and Settings › Account showed a signed-in account. The download has none of these: no accounts, no credits, nothing hosted. The account menu now reads "Free plan · no account", as in the download. Tests: `demo.test.ts` and `demoUi.test.tsx` (F-WB-01).
- D-811: The demo build never inlines fonts (`build.assetsInlineLimit` in `vite.demo.config.ts`, `src/demo/assets.ts`). The site's CSP for `/demo/app/` is `font-src 'self'`. Vite inlined JetBrains Mono's Cyrillic-extended subset (under its 4 KB limit) as a `data:` URI, so the live 0.3.0 demo logged a CSP refusal on every load. That had no visible effect, since the demo shows no Cyrillic text. The CSP stays strict; the desktop app's own CSP allows `data:` fonts and is unchanged. Found by walking the live site; reproduced locally by serving `out/` with `vercel.json`'s headers. Shipped to `main` as a hotfix, without the unreleased work on `product-m0-m5`.
- D-812: Local data controls (F-TL-05). (1) The export writes every SQLite table as `<table>.json`. Tables added later, such as M10's teammates and M11's routines, are therefore included without code changes: `threads.json` and its `thread_messages.json`, `dictionary_rules.json`, and so on. SQLite's own tables and the FTS indexes are left out, and blobs come out as `{"hex": …}`. It adds `teammates/<id>/{memory,skills}/`, `tasks/`, `scratchpad/` (notes are the user's work too) and a `manifest.json` that names what's left out. It never reads `providers/` (engine sign-ins; CLAUDE.md forbids touching vendor tokens), `auth/` (Vibezeo's encrypted sign-in), the keychain, logs, caches, backups, the browser profile or scrollback. Symbolic links are skipped. The zip is built as `.zip.part` and renamed into place, so a failed export leaves nothing half-written. (2) Delete local data runs at the next start, before `$DATA` is opened or logged to. The command closes terminals, forgets Vibezeo's keychain secrets (the sign-in key, so none outlive the data they protected), leaves `.delete-pending` and relaunches. A marker that can't be read keeps engine sign-ins. Deleting under a live SQLite WAL and open logs would be fragile. (3) Settings › Privacy › Your data: Export, Delete (danger, typed "delete", "Also remove engine sign-ins" off, the body says what the box changes) and, only for a signed-in hosted account, Delete my account. That one opens `{web}/account` through `billing_open`'s new `account` target. `ConfirmDialog` gained a `children` slot for the checkbox. (4) The demo answers both commands with "The demo keeps nothing…" rather than pretending to export. (5) The export dialog waits on the user, so its call has a 10-minute timeout.
- D-813: The server carries out "Delete account" (F-TL-05, the web's `DELETE /v1/me`).
  - At the request: the user's own subscription and those of Teams they own alone are cancelled first, so a billing failure changes nothing and the user can try again. Then the account is marked, every desktop sign-in and web session is revoked, and no new session is created for it, by any way in, SSO included ("This account is being deleted, so it can't be signed in to."). Signing in again would otherwise let someone use, or pay for, an account that is about to go.
  - The owner of a Team with other members gets 409 `TEAM_HAS_MEMBERS` ("Transfer it or remove them first"); deleting them would strand the members' plan. A Team that gains members after the request holds the purge until it's empty again.
  - `jobs/deletionJob` runs hourly under a Redis lock and purges requests older than 29 days, a day inside the promised 30. It deletes the Stripe customers (the user's and their Teams': the billing details, and any subscription started since), then in one transaction the Teams they own (members, policy, SSO), invites from and to them, device codes, dictionary sync, phone pairings, voice sessions, AI request records, daily usage, billing customer rows and the user (sessions, accounts, sign-in links, refresh tokens, desktop login codes and memberships cascade).
  - Money records stay: the append-only credit ledger, reservations, buckets, subscriptions, member usage and Team audit events, keyed by an id that no longer leads to a person. A customer Stripe no longer has counts as deleted, so a retried purge goes through; any other failure skips that account until the next pass without holding up the rest. The job logs counts only.
- D-814: Find work (F-CN-05) ranks on this machine (`tandem_core::find_work`).
  - Words meet at a light stem ("tests", "testing" and "tested" are "test"; "string" stays whole), 16 groups of terms that mean the same in software work are joined (login, auth and sign-in; test and spec; UI and frontend; docs and README; billing, payment and Stripe; bug, fix and error; deploy and release; …), and one typo is forgiven (two from 8 letters). Filler like "where's the agent doing" is dropped.
  - Each query word earns the most it can in a candidate's name (exact 3, same meaning 2, prefix 2, typo 1.5) or in its other text (1, 0.6, 0.6, 0.5), and the score is the average. One clear match (at least 1.5, with the next under two thirds of it) is gone to; otherwise up to 3 close ones are offered.
  - ARCHITECTURE named `nucleo-matcher`. Its subsequence matching suits abbreviations typed into a picker: it finds "ui" inside "build" and knows nothing of meaning, so it isn't used, and no dependency is added.
  - Candidates: open panes of workspaces that aren't removed (name, else title, engine or kind; then title, engine, folder, a browser's page and the last 20 lines of output) and threads that aren't archived (title and last message). Output and messages are redacted first. A match says what it was last doing: an agent's reported activity, else how a shell's last command ended (shell integration), else the last line on screen. Teammates join with M10's chats.
  - The palette: "Find work…" (`work.find`, no default keys) turns it into the search. Typing waits 120 ms, rows show the name and what it was doing, and choosing one shows the pane (workspace, tab, focus and the F-CD-12 flash) or opens the thread. Closing it goes back to commands. `CommandPalette` gained `shouldFilter`, `onQueryChange` and `resetKey`, and doesn't record searches as recent commands.
  - The hosted ranking (`/v1/voice/resolve`, 1 credit) isn't wired, because the endpoint doesn't exist yet. When it does, it runs only behind a new opt-in setting (off by default) with its "What leaves this machine" row, and this ranking stays the fallback. The demo says Find work searches the user's own terminals and threads, rather than pretending.
- D-815: A theme or contrast switch is instant. `ThemeProvider` marks `<html>` with `data-theme-switching` for two frames when the theme or contrast changes, and `base.css` turns transitions off under it. Before, each element faded through its own `transition-colors`, so for a moment the page was part-way between themes: E2E-M16-5's axe sweep in the full suite caught the title bar's mode buttons at #8a98ab on #f8fafc (2.8:1), a colour that exists in neither theme, because its colour-settle wait sampled only the body and dialogs.
- D-816: An early-access build's status bar shows no connection state. The entitlement's `online` flag starts true and only turns false after a refresh fails on the network; a build with no hosted service never refreshes, so it said "Online" with nothing to be online to, and kept saying it with no network at all (seen in the 0.4.0 offline smoke test). The status bar now shows Online or Offline only when `auth.hosted` is true; the version stays.
- D-817: E2E serves Dictate's models on port 8792. M8 (D-514) and M12 (D-954, D-959) each gave port 8791 to a server in `e2e/run.ts`, the models server and `mock-plugins`, built in parallel. Merged, the runner would find the port taken and stop before any spec ran. The models server moved: its port lives only in `run.ts` and `scripts/serve-models.ts`, while `mock-plugins` is pinned in Rust (`catalog.rs`, `mock_plugins.rs`).
- D-818: Routine and task runs name themselves to the gateway before their first message: `routine:<routine-id>` and `task:<issue-id>:<attempt-id>` (`AppRoutineHost` and `AppExecHost` hold the gateway). So a plugin write an unattended run holds for review is filed under that routine or task in Held actions. This is M12's hook (BUILD_LOG M12 item 7), which M11, built in parallel, couldn't call. Tests: `f_pl_08_a_routine_run_files_held_drafts_under_its_routine` and `f_pl_08_a_task_run_files_held_drafts_under_its_task_and_attempt`. Teammate mail runs (M10) don't name themselves yet, so a plugin write from one is refused ("this run doesn't say where it came from") rather than held; that fails closed, and M12's follow-up names them.
- D-819: One tray for Dictate and routines (F-SH-13). M8 built a tray for Dictate's status, and M11 one for background routines, in parallel, both under the id `main`. Merged, M11's `sync` removed the tray whenever no background runner was installed, which is always on Free, so Dictate's tray never showed. If routines were on, `sync` took M8's tray for its own and never added "Pause routines". `crate::tray` now owns the one tray: it shows while Dictate or background routines are on (PRODUCT_SPEC: "when Dictate or background routines are enabled"), with Open Vibezeo, the Dictate status line (opens Settings › Dictate), "Pause routines" only while background routines are on, and Quit Vibezeo. `services::tray` keeps the status line and reports changes. Tests: `f_sh_13_the_tray_shows_while_dictate_or_background_routines_are_on` and `f_sh_13_pause_routines_is_in_the_menu_only_with_background_routines`. Found by a read-only audit of the merged tree. Building the one tray is serialized: two startup refreshes (Dictate's and the routines') on different threads each found no tray and built one, and the second registration failed on D-Bus ("already exported", 64 times in a full E2E run, the run in which E2E-M3-9 and E2E-M9-1 timed out); with the lock there are none, and both specs pass.
- D-820: SSH and WSL threads start with no local stdio servers. M11 gave `ChatStart` a `stdio_servers` list (the tasks server, F-TK-02), and M14, built in parallel, added the two `ChatStart`s for threads on an SSH host or in WSL. Merged, those didn't compile. They get an empty list, as they already get no MCP config: the tasks server runs on this machine, where a remote engine can't start it. Terminal panes were already right, since remote and WSL panes branch off before the tasks wiring.
- D-821: E2E-M3-9's time budget grows with the command registry. F-SH-09.4 runs every registry command through the palette, about 1.4 to 2.2 s each (timed per command); with 43 commands it needed more than the suite's default 60 s. It timed out in full runs and then alone, after 41 commands in 59 s, with no command slow or stuck. The suite now allows 60 s plus 3 s per registry command. Each command still has to run from the palette within its own 3 s, so the test checks exactly what it did.
- D-822: E2E-M9-1's conflict test waits for the dialog to settle before clicking Compare. In two of three full runs the conflict dialog appeared as it should, so the app never overwrote silently. The test clicked Compare while the dialog was still zooming in, the click missed (the dialog showed no diff, and focus was on its × button), and the wait for the diff timed out. It passed alone. `settled()` is the helper other specs use for the same race (D-52).
- D-823: Diagnostics shows a remote terminal's spawn time as how long its host took to start it. M15 gave every PTY a spawn time for Help › Diagnostics (NFR §5.7), measured around opening the PTY and starting the program, and M14, built in parallel, added `spawn_remote` for terminals on an SSH host, where nothing local is opened or started. Merged, the remote session had no spawn time and the workspace didn't compile. `spawn_remote` now takes `spawn_ms`, and the remote service times the host's `pty.spawn` request, so the number covers the same work as a local one: starting the program, plus the round trip to the host.
- D-824: The credits page prices Cloud dictation (F-DI-05) and Enhance or Polish (F-DI-10) again. M15 made the page list only features ticked in REMAINING, and took those two rows off while they waited for M8, with a test that they stayed off until then. M8 shipped both (#41), so the merge put the rows back, with their prices from plans.ts, and the test now checks they're listed. Every row is still checked against REMAINING.
- D-825: The Dictate widget's pinned permissions grow from 7 to 10, after review. M15's least-privilege test (§5.3) pins exactly what the widget window may call, so that anything more is a reviewed change; M8's F-DI-05 and F-DI-10, built in parallel, gave the widget three more: `allow-dictate-use-local` ("Use local for this take" after a cloud failure), `allow-dictate-quick-action` (Enhance and Polish) and `core:event:allow-emit-to` (See plans, an event to the main window). Each backs a control the widget shows, and the widget renders only the app's own bundled code, so the test now pins all ten. The main window and webview rules are unchanged.

### M16 · coordinating session log
- F-SH-12: the store, the commands, `NoteEditor`, the Scratchpad tab and E2E-M16-5. The acceptance test types without a pause (a timestamp every 25 ms through the browser's own text input path), kills the app with `kill -9` within 1 s, and checks the newest saved timestamp on disk is at most 450 ms old (300 ms, one keystroke and the write); it then checks the note is listed after a restart. 3 of 3 runs green. Found on the way: the browser demo answers commands it doesn't know with `null`, so its New note would have put `null` in the list and crashed the page; the demo now has the scratchpad (its test failed before).
- F-SH-09 (release builds): while checking that `Mod+U` underlines in a note, found that the webview-key suppression ran before any editor saw the key (D-804). A dispatcher test with the real `CodeEditor` and `NoteEditor` in a release-mode boot failed for that reason and passed in a debug-mode one; it passes in both now.
- F-SH-09 P2 (rebinding): the setting, `HotkeyRecorder`, the Shortcuts page, the cheatsheet and E2E-M16-5 shortcuts. Found on the way:
  - Adding every command title to the page's filter keywords made Settings' filter jump to Shortcuts for "diagnostics". F-TL-04.2's test caught it, and the keywords are generic now.
  - E2E-M3-9 named its palette test F-SH-09.6, but it covers AC4; it's renamed.
  - The E2E reset now clears `shortcut_overrides`, so one spec's rebinding can't reach the next.
- F-TL-05 slice 2 (account purge): the job, the request's new checks and E2E-M1-6. Found on the way: the request marked the account before cancelling the subscription, so a Stripe failure left a half-deleted account (now billing goes first); a solo Team owner's Team subscription kept billing for 29 days although the page says "cancels your subscription now"; and a deleted user could sign straight back in.
- F-CN-05 (local): the ranking, the candidates, the palette mode and E2E-M16-2. Checking the ranking by hand before running it caught a text-only match that counted as strong, and a typo of a same-meaning term ("documntation") that found nothing.
- F-SH-05 (D-815): the full E2E regression for 0.4.0 failed E2E-M16-5's light-theme axe sweep (also seen by M10) while the spec passed on its own: the title bar was caught mid-transition after the theme switch. Theme switches are now instant; `theme.test.tsx` covers the switch and the CSS rule.

## M16 · F-AC-07 Usage dashboard
Built on branch `m16-usage-dashboard` (from `product-m0-m5`) by the M13 session as part of its follow-on chain. Decisions come from the M13 range (D-600–D-699).
- [x] F-AC-07 Usage dashboard. `tandem_engines::usage_log` reads Claude Code's `projects/**/*.jsonl` and Codex's `sessions/**/*.jsonl` through the registry's `usage_log_globs`, only inside each account's provider home. It streams line by line, and skips and counts a line that isn't JSON, is over 4 MB, or has no usable date or token counts. Only numbers, dates, ids and the working folder are read; no message text is kept. `services::usage_report` turns the scan into 12 weeks of days in the user's zone, totals per engine and per account, and the top 8 workspaces (a folder counts for the workspace whose root holds it). `usage_report` and `usage_share_save` are declared in ARCHITECTURE §3. Workspace menu → **Usage…** opens `UsageDialog`, which has empty, loading, error and populated states, a `Heatmap` (new in `@tandem/ui`, tokens only), engine, account and workspace lists, "N lines skipped", and a share card. The card is drawn on a canvas, shows only engines with usage, and leaves out workspace names unless the user switches them on. Tests: `usage_log` 6 (`f_ac_07_1`–`3` and 3 more), the report 1, `Heatmap` 1, the page 4, and E2E-M16-6 (F-AC-07.1, F-AC-07.2 and a saved PNG).

### M16 · F-AC-07 decisions
- D-615: The planned commands `usage_dashboard` (an op) and `usage_share_card` (a PNG in `$DATA/tmp`) became `usage_report` (a plain reply that runs on the blocking pool while the page shows its loading state; a progress op can come later if large log folders make it slow) and `usage_share_save`. The webview draws the card because it already has the numbers, fonts and tokens; the core checks the bytes are a PNG under 10 MB and writes them where the user picks in the save dialog, so no copy is left in app data. Debug builds read the save path from the file named by `TANDEM_FAKE_SAVE_PATH`, as the folder picker's fake does.
- D-616: A day's tokens are input plus output plus cache (Claude's cache reads and writes; Codex's cached input is counted once, as cache, and its reasoning tokens as output). Claude lines are deduplicated by `message.id:requestId`, because Claude Code writes one assistant message across several lines with the same usage. Dates are RFC 3339 only and are bucketed in the user's zone; a line without one is skipped.
- D-617: An engine whose registry entry has no `usage_log_globs` is listed under `unsupported`, and the page says its usage isn't shown, instead of showing it as zero.

### M16 · F-AC-07 log
- F-AC-07: the parser, the report, the two commands, `Heatmap`, the page, the share card and E2E-M16-6, which passed on its first run. While writing the page test I found that "1.5K" isn't how `tokenCount` rounds (it shows "2K"), so the test fixtures use round thousands. The E2E writes three days of Claude log lines (1K, 2K and 3K tokens) and a broken line into a test account's home. It checks "6K tokens", the last three heatmap levels, the engine and workspace lists, "1 line skipped", and a PNG saved through the fake save dialog.

## M16 · F-AU-01 Auto
Built on branch `m16-auto` by the M13 session as part of its follow-on chain. It is stacked on `m13-hosted-services` (D-618), and its decisions come from the M13 range (D-600–D-699).
- [x] F-AU-01 Auto (Pro, credits).
  - **API.** `POST /v1/auto/next` (`routes/auto.ts`, `services/ai.ts` `autoNext`) runs one metered turn at 6 credits (remote-config price `auto_turn`), uses the per-mode supervisor instruction, and holds the answer to `{action, text|reason}` (`parseAutoNext`). It needs Pro (`requirePlan(auto)`), refuses a Team whose policy has `disableAuto` (403 `FORBIDDEN_POLICY`), applies the existing 30/min limit, and refuses `turn ≥ ceiling` before any model call. An unusable answer refunds the turn, and the goal and scrollback are never logged. `MockAi` answers `prompt "Continue with the next step."` until the scrollback shows `ALL TESTS PASSED`, then `done` (ARCHITECTURE §14.8).
  - **Desktop.** `services::auto::AutoService` is the loop in §8.8. It sits behind an `AutoHost` trait (pane, status, tail, typing, API, notification), so every rule is unit-tested with a fake host and a write log.
  - **Commands.** `auto_start`, `auto_list`, `auto_pause`, `auto_resume`, `auto_stop` and `auto_dismiss` are declared in ARCHITECTURE §3. `auto_start` checks for an agent pane with its agent running, the policy, the plan and the consent.
  - **UI.** Pane `⋯` → **Auto…** opens `AutoDialog`, where you pick a mode (Custom takes a goal and 1–30 turns) and see the consent step until "Don't ask again". The header chip `AutoChip` reads "⚡ Finish · 3/20" with a spinning ring while live; it has Pause, Resume and Stop, and it shows Done, Stuck or Stopped with the reason until you dismiss it.
  - **Tests.**
    - API: 9, `test/auto.test.ts` (F-AU-01.4 included).
    - Supervisor: 12 (`f_au_01_1`–`5`, plus retries, end-reason mapping, pane close and engine change, stop and pause, and start checks).
    - UI: 4.
    - E2E-M16-7: 5 tests (consent and a Custom run to its ceiling, F-AU-01.5 approval pause, done, F-AU-01.2 engine exit, F-AU-01.4 no credits).

### M16 · F-AU-01 decisions
- D-618: Auto is stacked on `m13-hosted-services`, as admin is (D-613). It needs M13's metering, plan limits and Team policy, and its PR targets that branch until #9 merges.
- D-619: A Team member whose admin turned Auto off ends with a 13th reason, `turned_off` ("Your organization turned Auto off."). PRODUCT_SPEC's list has no reason for this, and "no subscription" would be untrue for a paying Team. The server enforces the policy too (403 `FORBIDDEN_POLICY`), so an outdated client can't get around it.
- D-620: "Idle at its prompt" compares status records, not times. A turn is ready on `Done`, `Idle`, `Failed` or `Waiting(Input)` only when that status record differs from the one the pane had when the last prompt was typed. Status times come from the app clock, which E2E runs can stop, so a time comparison could wait forever. `Waiting(Question)` pauses like an approval, because it asks a person. `type_prompt` now refuses only a pane waiting on an approval or a question: an agent waiting for input is exactly where Auto types. F-EN-06 AC2's case is the permission dialog, which still refuses with "<name> is waiting for your approval".
- D-621: Runs live in memory, and the `auto_runs` table stays unused. A restart restarts the pane's engine, which would end the run as engine exited anyway. Pause takes effect at the next turn. Stop takes effect at once: nothing more is typed, even if an answer is on its way, and that answer's 6 credits are spent. 429 is retried like an unreachable service (1 s, 3 s, 9 s, then `api_unreachable`). Typing that fails 3 turns in a row ends the run as stuck, so a pane that won't take input can't keep spending credits.
- D-622: The consent dialog shows whenever `auto_consent` or `auto_dont_ask` is off. Continue turns `auto_consent` on, and "Don't ask again" turns `auto_dont_ask` on. Turning the Settings → Privacy switch off withdraws consent: `auto_start` refuses (PermissionDenied) and the dialog asks again.
- D-623: An ended run raises a pane notification through the usual channels. Done and turn ceiling use the `done` kind and the other reasons use `failed`. Nothing is raised when the user stopped the run. The body is the supervisor's reason or "Mode · n of m turns", redacted.

### M16 · F-AU-01 log
- F-AU-01: the route, the supervisor, the commands, the dialog, the chip and E2E-M16-7.
  - Results: 5 of 5 in each of the last 2 runs, and E2E-M4-5 still passes with the narrower `type_prompt` check (D-620).
  - The first run found three test problems, none in the app:
    - The fake engine logs what it reads as hex, so the spec now counts prompts from that PTY write log.
    - The header chip is clipped at the pane's width, so the spec reads its `textContent`.
    - The scrollback still held a previous test's `ALL TESTS PASSED`, so the mock supervisor rightly said done; the done test now runs last, on its own pane.
  - The run to its ceiling spends exactly 3 × 6 credits, and its end raises "Auto reached its turn limit".

## M16 · F-BL-06 P2 Team SSO
Built on branch `m16-team-sso` by the M13 session, as the coordinator added to its chain. It is stacked on `m13-hosted-services`, and its decisions come from the M13 range.
- [x] F-BL-06 SSO (P2).
  - **API.**
    - `providers/sso`: an `SsoProvider` interface with three implementations.
      - `OidcSso` handles any OpenID Connect provider: discovery, authorization code with PKCE S256 and a nonce, and an ID token checked with jose for signature, issuer, audience, expiry and nonce. It falls back to userinfo when the token has no email.
      - `WorkOsSso` handles SAML through WorkOS.
      - `MockSso` is the test identity provider.
    - `services/sso.ts` covers the connection, the domains, sign-in and enforcement. Its admin routes sit under `/v1/orgs/:id/sso`; `/v1/sso/lookup` and `/v1/sso/start` are public.
    - The return leg `GET /v1/auth/sso/callback` is a better-auth plugin endpoint. It finds or creates the account, joins the org and sets an ordinary better-auth session cookie.
    - Migration `0002_m16_team_sso` adds `org_sso` and `org_sso_domains`. WorkOS is in the subprocessor list.
  - **Web.**
    - `/signin` gets "Sign in with SSO" and explains each `sso_error` code.
    - `/team` gets a "Single sign-on" card for owners and admins: domains with their TXT record and Check DNS, the protocol, the OIDC fields (the secret is never shown again) or the WorkOS organization, the redirect URI to register, "Require SSO" and Remove.
    - Audit labels are added for the SSO events.
  - **Desktop.** No change. The desktop signs in through the web page (F-BE-02), which now offers SSO. That path is not covered by an E2E yet.
  - **Tests.**
    - API: 12 tests in `test/sso.test.ts`. They run a real OIDC flow against an in-process provider with a real RS256 key. They also cover a forged token, an unverified address, an address outside the domains, a replayed state, a denial, seats, another org, enforcement and the WorkOS adapter.
    - Web E2E-M16-8: 3 tests. All 15 web E2E pass.

### M16 · F-BL-06 P2 decisions
- D-624: SSO is our own small module behind `SsoProvider`, not the `@better-auth/sso` package. Connections are per org and change at runtime, and the domain proof and seat rules are ours anyway. Only the callback lives inside better-auth (as a plugin endpoint), so sessions and cookies stay better-auth's. Like admin (D-613), this branch is stacked on `m13-hosted-services` because it needs M13's orgs, seats and audit.
- D-625: OIDC is spoken directly, and SAML goes through WorkOS. Parsing and verifying SAML's signed XML ourselves is a known source of account-takeover bugs. WorkOS needs `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` and `WORKOS_API_KEY` (HUMAN_TODO). Until they are set, the Team page shows SAML as not available and the API refuses it (`SSO_UNAVAILABLE`).
- D-626: The OIDC client secret is stored sealed with AES-256-GCM, using a key derived with HKDF from `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`. No API response contains it (`hasSecret` only), and saving without a secret keeps the stored one. Rotating `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` means admins re-enter their secrets. CLAUDE.md's keychain rule is about the desktop; this secret lives on the server.
- D-627: An org proves an email domain with a DNS TXT record (`_vibezeo-sso.<domain>` = `vibezeo-sso=<token>`).
  - Its provider can sign in only addresses on its verified domains, so an org's identity provider can't assert someone else's address and take over their account.
  - Only one org can verify a domain (a partial unique index).
  - An address the provider marks `email_verified: false` is refused.
- D-628: The first SSO sign-in creates the account with its email verified and joins the org as a member if a seat is free (just-in-time). An existing account with that address is the same person and joins. An account already in another org is refused, so the one-org-per-user rule stays. Each sign-in is in the org's audit log.
- D-629: "Require SSO" needs a verified domain.
  - For those addresses, password, magic-link and sign-up requests answer 403 `SSO_REQUIRED` with the reason.
  - A session-create database hook refuses every other way in (Google, the auto sign-in after email verification) except the SSO callback.
  - It applies only while the org's Team plan is active, so a lapsed plan can't lock members out.
  - Removing the last verified domain turns it off.
- D-630: The test identity provider (`provider: "mock"`) exists only with test routes on.
  - `/__test/sso` sets an org up with a verified domain.
  - `/__test/idp/next` picks who signs in, and `/__test/idp/authorize` hands the code back.
  - The web E2E API now runs with `API_PUBLIC_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8787` so the browser can reach that page.

### M16 · F-BL-06 P2 log
- F-BL-06 SSO: the provider interface, the service, the plugin callback, enforcement, the web pages, 12 API tests and E2E-M16-8. Both suites passed on their first run.
  - The only fix was in my own code: the error redirect went to `/sign-in`, but the page is `/signin`. Reading the web app before writing its E2E caught it.
  - The full web E2E suite (15) passes. The M13 Team specs still pass with the new card on `/team`.
  - The gate's web typecheck then caught that a Next.js page may not export anything but its page. The error messages moved to `lib/sso.ts`, and E2E-M16-8 passed again afterwards.

## M12 checklist (MILESTONES M12)
Built on branch `m12-plugins-approvals` (from `m10-teammates-gateway`, because M12 builds on M10's gateway), in parallel with M10, M11 and the other milestones (D-950). Owns F-PL-02…09; completes F-SH-13 (`plugin/callback`), F-AG-03 (the nudge) and F-AG-05 (per-plugin toggles) with real plugins, F-TH-07 (Attach ▾ → "Plugins for this thread", UI-94), F-DB-01 (plugin approvals on cards) and F-TL-02 (the filter of the user's credentials). This section holds M12's state, decisions (from D-950) and log.
- [x] 1. Vendor modules (F-PL-02). `vendors::VendorManifest` declares id, label, category, a two-letter tile (`logo`, no vendor artwork), description, `auth` (+ `alt_auth`, e.g. GitHub's token), `upstream` (`RemoteMcp{urls}` / `Rest{base}` / `LocalPort` / `Builtin`), pinned hosts, auth hosts, status, risk rules (globs on the tool name, optionally per connection mode), target keys, the key hint and scopes. `VendorModule` gets `tools`, `call`, `verify`, `describe`, `pre_execute` and `revoke`, each with a `CallCtx` (the connection and its `PinnedClient`). Two generic modules carry the catalog: `mcp::McpModule` (the gateway as a Streamable HTTP MCP client: initialize, paginated `tools/list` cached 10 min per connection, `tools/call` to the server that listed the tool, a session reopened once when it expires) and `rest::RestModule` (declared tools that build requests under the pinned base). `classify` gives the first matching rule's class and `unclassified` otherwise. Tests: the declaration test over the whole registry (`f_pl_02_1`, also checks every upstream URL is pinned and every key-taking module says where to get a key) and `f_pl_02_2`.
- [x] 2. Credentials (F-PL-04). `credentials::CredentialStore` keeps `{access_token, refresh_token?, expires_at?, token_type, client_secret?}` or `{api_key}` in the keychain under `<NS>.plugins` / `<plugin>:<connection-id>`, refuses more than 16 KB, never prints a secret (`Debug`), zeroizes on drop and registers every secret it reads or writes with the shared redactor (F-TL-02). `pinned::PinnedClient` attaches the credential only on an exact scheme + host + port + path-prefix match, follows at most 5 redirects by hand, drops the credential on every hop off the pin, refuses hops to private, loopback and link-local addresses that aren't pinned, never lets a caller set its own `Authorization`, and redacts every secret of the connection out of the response. The gateway's call path loads the credential per call, refreshes OAuth tokens a minute before expiry or after a 401 (single-flight per connection), and on a failed refresh sets Needs attention once and returns "{Plugin} needs to be reconnected in Settings › Plugins". Tests: `f_pl_04_1`, `f_pl_04_2`, `f_pl_04_3` (one notification over three calls), the transparent refresh, `invariant_rule_3_pinned_hosts_only`, the 16 KB cap and `f_tl_02_saved_credentials_are_redacted_everywhere`.
- [x] 3. Connect flows (F-PL-03, UI-90/91/92). Settings › Plugins (after Teammates) is `features/plugins`: the searchable card grid with categories (`/` search, 1–7 category), Connected first with account and teammate count, Preview dimmed with Connect disabled, Blocked by org and Free's "Pro" with their reason; the connect sheet (OAuth steps live from `plugins:connect`, the key masked and tested, "needs no sign-in" for Web fetch, the failing step named with the provider's words untruncated, "Still waiting after 5 minutes." on timeout, then "Connected as …" and the teammates to enable, none checked); the plugin page (UI-92: KeyValue, Teammates/Approvals/Tools tabs, Reconnect, Disconnect… with UI-92's confirm). `services::plugins` runs the flows in the app: OAuth in the background with progress per step, cancel (`plugin_connect_cancel`), the API key, local port or no sign-in, disconnect (revoke, credential, connection and its grants, leases, remembered approvals), Needs attention with one "Reconnect {plugin}" notification. `tandem://plugin/callback` reaches the same loopback listener (F-SH-13). Tests: the gateway's OAuth tests (AC1–AC4), `plugins.test.tsx` (UI-90/91, the early-failure race), E2E-M12-1/2.
- [x] 4. Grants (F-PL-05). Per teammate (the plugin page's Teammates tab, the connect sheet's last step, the nudge and teammate settings, which link "Connect more plugins…" to Settings › Plugins), per thread (Attach ▾ → "Plugins for this thread…", D-957), per workspace (read by the launcher and the execution re-check) and "Grant these plugins to all threads". The Team allowlist is the entitlement's `policy.allowedPlugins` (a test fixture through `test_plugin_allowlist`): a blocked plugin's card says why and `plugin_connect_*` refuse it. Tests: `f_pl_05_1_a_new_teammate_has_no_plugin_grants_with_three_connected`, the store's per-subject test, `plugins.test.tsx`, E2E-M12-3/6.
- [x] 5. Policy engine (F-PL-06). `policy::decide(class, mode, options)` is the ARCHITECTURE §11.7 table (allow, ask with or without the conversation scope, hold); `Mode::of` reads attended + access. Remembered "for this conversation" approvals are keyed by conversation, plugin, tool, argument shape and literal target, in memory (cleared on quit, sign-out, reconnect, disconnect). Spend approvals are bound to `approval_hash(tool, canonical args, amount + currency)`, and right before a spend or credit call runs the module's `pre_execute` re-reads the cost; a different hash is refused ("The amount changed from 5.00 USD to 7.00 USD"). After any approval the lease, the grant (asked of the app) and the connection are checked again. The gateway asks the app through `hooks::PluginHooks` (card, hold, grant re-check, needs attention). Tests: `f_pl_06_1_policy_table` (21 cases), the options, `f_pl_06_2`, `f_pl_06_3`, the remember key, the re-checks (grant, connection and lease each removed while the card is up), expiry and deny.
- [x] 5a. `invariant_rule_4_unattended_never_approves` (with M7's `invariant_rule_4_unattended_never_full`): for each access level, every ask-class tool from an unattended session is held without a card, even with a remembered approval and arguments claiming the user allowed it; only the read runs.
- [x] 6. Approval card (F-PL-07, UI-93). A plugin approval's `detail` is `PluginApprovalDetail`; the thread renders it as the plugin card (plugin, risk badge, action, account, target, cost in the danger tone for spend, "Full request" behind a toggle), Approve / "Allow for the rest of this chat" (scope `conversation`, only where the gateway offers it) / Deny; the pinned card and the Dashboard card say the same (plugin, risk, cost; "Approve"). The card's request masks the connection's own secrets even before this run has read them (`f_pl_07_1`, D-958). Expiry after 30 min is the gateway's (`f_pl_07_2`).
- [x] 7. Held drafts (F-PL-08). `held_drafts` (display `D-<n>`, `claim` moves `held` → `executing` once), Settings › Plugins › Held actions (each draft with where it came from, Approve & run, Edit & run in the code editor checked against the tool's schema, Discard, bulk run and discard), one "N actions held for review" notification per run, `held:changed`. A run says where it came from through `GatewayService::set_unattended_origin` (routine and task runs since D-818; `test_unattended_run` stands in for E2E). Tests: `f_pl_08_1` (0 upstream requests), the store's run-once test, the schema test, `plugins.test.tsx`, E2E-M12-5.
- [x] 8. Catalog (F-PL-09). `vendors::catalog`: GitHub, Linear, Notion, Sentry, Vercel, Cloudflare (bindings + observability servers in one module), Supabase, Stripe and PostHog over their remote MCP servers; Slack (bot tools) and Gmail over REST; the built-in Web fetch (read-only, robots.txt, private ranges refused also after redirects, 2 MB); nine previews (Google Ads, Meta Ads, X, YouTube, Shopify, QuickBooks, RevenueCat, Resend, Figma) that list but can't connect. Each upstream and auth kind was checked against the vendor's docs and a live unauthenticated request (D-953). `tests/vendors.rs` runs a fixture contract per ready module against wiremock (sign-in only at the pinned upstream, the account label, every listed tool's class, a call, the echoed token redacted, no access without the credential) plus Web fetch's robots.txt, and a test that every ready module has one. Debug E2E builds point every upstream and sign-in host at one local mock (`TANDEM_E2E_PLUGIN_BASE`, `catalog::rebase`).
- [x] Exit. G green · every ready module has a fixture test (`tests/vendors.rs`) · the policy table has 21 cases (`f_pl_06_1`). On `m12-plugins-approvals` at 8af4298, with `product-m0-m5` merged in at 77a4fb0: the full desktop E2E, 88 of 88 spec files, including E2E-M12-1…6. The coordinating session merged the base again (1257fcb): G green, and E2E `m12-*`, `m10-*`, `m7-2`, `m5-4`, `m3-9` and `m16-2` passed, 18 of 18 spec files. Merged as pull request #34 (d5a4029). HUMAN_TODO keeps the manual vendor smoke tests and the OAuth app registrations.

### M12 decisions
- D-950: M12 is built in parallel with M10 and M11 at the owner's request, although MILESTONES lists M11 as its prerequisite. It starts from `m10-teammates-gateway` (the gateway) and builds against M10's and M11's declared interfaces where they aren't there yet (noted in the log); its decisions are numbered D-950 to D-999.
- D-951: Vendor module shape. `VendorManifest` gains `logo` (a two-letter tile like EngineGlyph, never vendor artwork, UI-90), `alt_auth` (GitHub, Slack, Linear, Supabase, Stripe and PostHog also take a token; the connect sheet offers both), `key_hint` and `scopes`; risk rules may name a connection mode (Stripe's test/live, from the key's prefix). A module with no rule for a tool classes it `unclassified`, and a Stripe connection whose mode isn't known (OAuth) gets only its read rules, so its writes ask every time. The built-in Web fetch carries no credential and pins nothing; every other module pins at least one host.
- D-952: Approvals and holds reach the app through `hooks::PluginHooks`; the gateway decides (policy, remember scope, spend re-check) and the app only shows cards, stores drafts and answers grant re-checks, so the rules live in one tested place. A plugin approval's card is a thread approval (`ApprovalKind::PluginAction`, or `Spend` with a cost) whose `detail` is the card's fields as JSON; its options are Once plus Conversation only for `write` and `external_publish`.
- D-953: Catalog choices from checking the vendors' docs and live metadata (October 2026). All eleven upstreams in ARCHITECTURE §11.8 answer as listed. Corrections: Stripe and PostHog are OAuth with DCR first, with their keys as the alternative (Stripe's from 31 October 2026 must be agent keys); Linear and Supabase also take personal tokens; GitHub issues are `issue_write`/`issue_read` (rules `*_write` write, `*_read` read added); Slack's `search.messages` needs a user token, so the bot tools are list channels, channel history and post message; Vercel registers only clients it has approved (HUMAN_TODO). Fixtures are written from the vendors' published tool lists, not recorded from live accounts; the smoke test against real accounts is manual (HUMAN_TODO).
- D-954: The mock authorization server (`crates/tandem-gateway/tests/support/mock_as.rs`) also serves a GitHub-shaped MCP upstream, and is built as the test-only `mock-plugins` binary (`--features test-vendors`) that the desktop E2E runner starts on port 8791, so the E2E suite and the Rust tests use one mock. F-PL-01 AC2's memory check moved to its own test binary (`tests/memory.rs`): it reads the process's resident memory, which the new wiremock and OAuth tests running beside it in the unit-test binary pushed to 185 MB.
- D-955: OAuth callbacks go to one loopback listener per app run (`oauth::Loopback`, `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/callback`), which hands a callback only to the attempt whose `state` it carries and that is still waiting; cancel, timeout or any failure stops the attempt waiting, so a late callback reaches the app and is ignored (F-PL-03 AC4) rather than hitting a closed port. `tandem://plugin/callback` feeds the same listener's `deliver`. A new credential is verified before it's saved and saved before success is shown; a reconnect replaces the old credential only after verification, and a failed verification leaves it untouched (AC3).

- D-956: The held-drafts event is `held:changed {originId, held}` (the run whose drafts changed and how many it still holds); the list re-reads `held_drafts_list` rather than carrying drafts, since a draft view needs the tool's schema from the gateway. ARCHITECTURE §3.3 says so.
- D-957: "Plugins for this thread" (F-TH-07, UI-94; UI_SPEC has no wireframe for it) is the composer's Attach ▾ menu ("Add files…", "Plugins for this thread…"; the paperclip opens it) and a small dialog of switches for the connected plugins, none on by default; plugins granted to all threads show on and locked. Teammate chats don't get it: a teammate's grants are its own (F-AG-05).
- D-958: The approval card's "Full request" masks the connection's own secrets explicitly (read from the keychain at ask time) as well as through the shared redactor, which only knows secrets this run has already read or saved. Found by `f_pl_07_1`, which writes the credential as an earlier run would have and fails without the explicit mask.
- D-959: E2E: `run.ts` builds and starts `mock-plugins` on :8791 (D-954) and sets `TANDEM_E2E_PLUGIN_BASE`; the scripted browser finishes a sign-in at the mock's `/authorize` by following its redirect to the app's loopback, or, when `plugin-signin.txt` says `hold`, writes the URL to `plugin-authorize.txt` so a spec can call back late (E2E-M12-2). The E2E reset disconnects every plugin (M10's specs left test connections behind, which would use up Free's one connection) and clears the allowlist fixture.

- D-960: One shape for plugin OAuth clients: the config's `plugins: {<id>: {client_id?, enabled}}` (ARCHITECTURE §14.10), as the API's signed config already had it; the desktop read `plugin_oauth_clients.<id>` (MILESTONES' wording), so a client id put where the API expects it was never used. `tandem_core::remote_config::PluginClient` is the one type (the API's `RemoteConfig.plugins` uses the generated TS type), and tests parse the API's and the bundled config with it. Each vendor module declares where its client comes from (`OAuthClient::Dynamic | Reviewed | Registered{public}`), and `vendors::availability` gives the ways this build can connect it from that and its config entry; the card carries them as `methods`, and a ready module with none is `unavailable` with the reason (Gmail: "needs an app registration that this build doesn't have yet"; Vercel: "hasn't approved Vibezeo's sign-in yet"), so it turns Ready once its client id is in the config. `enabled: false` turns a plugin off without a release. A configured client id is used before dynamic registration (Vercel's approved client). In debug E2E builds the one mock registers every client, so rebased modules are `Dynamic`.
- D-961: Which hand-registered clients can sign in from the desktop, checked against the vendors' docs (October 2026). GitHub's code exchange requires `client_secret` even with PKCE; its device flow doesn't, so GitHub's browser sign-in waits for a device-flow implementation and GitHub is token-only (fine-grained PAT) meanwhile, client id or not. Slack takes PKCE without a secret, but a localhost redirect from a PKCE app counts as a desktop redirect, and desktop redirects can't request bot scopes, which the Slack module needs: Slack's sign-in will exchange the code through the API (secret kept there), and Slack is token-only (`xoxb-`) meanwhile. Google lists `client_secret` as optional for installed apps with PKCE, so Gmail is `Registered{public: true}` and offers the browser sign-in once its client id is configured; HUMAN_TODO has the check. None of this is claimed to work before then.
- D-962: The redirect a vendor sees is always `http://127.0.0.1:<random port>/callback` (D-955). `tandem://plugin/callback` only hands a callback to that listener (F-SH-13) and is never registered with a vendor; HUMAN_TODO's Slack line named it and `https://app.vibezeo.com/plugin/callback` and now names the API route D-961 plans.
- D-963: A teammate mail run is filed with the gateway as `mail:<mail-id>` when the mail is delivered (`AppMailHost::deliver`, which now has the gateway), not when its chat is woken: an unattended chat can take several mails of one conversation, and each turn's held drafts should name the mail that started it. Before, mail runs named no origin, so a plugin write from one was refused ("this run doesn't say where it came from") instead of held. Routine and task runs are filed by M11's hosts (D-818).
### M12 log
- M12 tasks 1, 2, 5, 5a and 8 (gateway level): vendor modules, the MCP and REST modules, credentials, `PinnedClient`, OAuth (discovery, DCR with the confidential fallback, PKCE, the loopback, exchange, refresh, revoke), connect orchestration, the policy and the call path with approvals, holds and re-checks, the catalog and its fixture tests, the mock authorization server. `cargo test -p tandem-gateway`: 55 unit, 13 vendor fixture, 8 OAuth and the memory test. Found on the way: a cancelled sign-in left its `state` registered, so a late callback was silently dropped instead of counted as ignored; every exit of the flow now stops the attempt waiting (`f_pl_03_4`). The vendor check corrected Stripe's and PostHog's auth and GitHub's issue tool names (D-953).
- M12 tasks 3, 4, 6 and 7 (app and UI), after merging `product-m0-m5` with M10 (#5): Settings › Plugins, the connect sheet and plugin page, the plugin approval card in threads and on the Dashboard, Held actions, Attach ▾ → "Plugins for this thread", and E2E-M12-1…6 (16 tests; `mock-plugins` started by the runner). Found on the way: a fast sign-in failure reached the sheet before the op id and was dropped (early events are now replayed); the approval card's request didn't mask a credential this run hadn't read yet (D-958); Preview and blocked cards were dimmed with opacity, which failed colour contrast (they use a quieter surface now); WebKitWebDriver's `getText` leaves out text in `overflow: hidden` boxes, so the specs read `textContent`. G green: 1,000 Rust and 970 TypeScript tests.
- M12 follow-up (D-960…D-963), before plugins ship in a download: the desktop now reads plugin clients where the API's config puts them; cards show what this build can actually connect (Gmail and Vercel "Not available yet" with the reason; GitHub and Slack token-only, with why); the connect paths refuse what isn't offered; HUMAN_TODO's registration steps match the redirect the app sends. Tests: `f_pl_09_plugin_clients_read_the_apis_config_shape`, `f_pl_09_shipped_configs_hold_only_well_formed_plugin_entries`, `f_pl_09_what_each_module_offers_follows_its_client_and_the_config`, `plugins.test.tsx` (the unavailable card, the token-only sheet). Mail runs are filed under their mail (D-963, `f_pl_08_a_mail_run_is_filed_under_its_mail`).

## M16 · F-BE-08 Conductor voice API
Built on branch `m16-voice-api` (from `product-m0-m5`), in `apps/api` only, while the desktop's Conductor client is built separately against ARCHITECTURE §12.6 and §14.6. Its decisions are numbered from D-1000. The pull request targets `product-m0-m5`, never `main`.
- [x] F-BE-08 voice session minting (P2), with the API halves of F-CN-02 (the doer) and F-CN-05 (Find work).
  - **Routes** (`routes/voice.ts`, `services/voice.ts`):
    - `POST /v1/voice/session` holds 10 minutes of credits (1,200 at the remote-config `voice_10s` price of 20), then mints a realtime session and returns `{session_id, provider, ephemeral_token, ws_url, expires_at, max_seconds: 1800}`. It needs Pro (`conductor`), refuses a Team whose policy has `disableConductor` (403 `FORBIDDEN_POLICY`), and allows 10 calls an hour. A provider failure returns the hold (503 `VOICE_UNAVAILABLE`).
    - `POST /v1/voice/session/:id/settle {seconds}` charges `ceil(s/10)×20`, refunds the rest and is idempotent, also when two settles race. It is never plan- or policy-gated, so a call that loses either mid-way still gets its hold back.
    - `POST /v1/voice/act` runs one doer round during a live call (409 `SESSION_ENDED` after it), on the Claude API through `AiProvider`, with the closed catalog as tools. No credits move: the doer is part of the call's rate. 60 a minute.
    - `POST /v1/voice/resolve` ranks Find-work candidates through `metered()` for 1 credit (the remote-config `resolve` price). 30 a minute.
  - **Jobs.** `jobs/voiceSweeper.ts` runs every 60 s under a Redis lock and settles calls 2 minutes past their cap.
  - **Ledger.** `reserve` takes an `expiresAt`; `topUp` and `settle(…, {topUp: true})` grow a held reservation as far as the balance and a Team member's cap allow.
  - **Providers.** `providers/realtime`: `RealtimeProvider.mint` with `MockRealtime` and `OpenAiRealtime` (the vendor's client-secret endpoint). `ClaudeAi` now sends tools, tool choice, effort, a JSON schema and a cached system prompt when a feature asks, and returns tool calls and the stop reason. `MockAi` maps keyword intents to doer tool calls and scores Find work by shared words (ARCHITECTURE §14.8).
  - **Data.** Migration `0003_m16_voice` adds `voice_sessions.provider_session_id`, `last_act_at` and an index on open calls. `apps/api/voice-tools.json` (written by `pnpm --filter api openapi`, checked by a test) is the doer's catalog for the desktop's ActionRegistry. `openapi.json` has the four routes.
  - **Tests.**
    - API: 27 in `test/voice.test.ts` (F-BE-08 AC1 and AC2, F-CN-01 AC1's 65 s = 140 credits, F-CN-02 AC2, F-CN-05 AC1, the sweeper, top-ups, 402, 403, 409, 429, and a log capture).
    - Catalog: 6 in `src/services/voiceTools.test.ts`. Providers: 4 for `OpenAiRealtime`, 2 more for `ClaudeAi`.
    - The hosted-route guard (`test/hosted.test.ts`) lists the new routes.
  - **Not in this branch.** The dev WebSocket mock `GET /v1/dev/realtime` (F-CN-01, D-1006) and everything in `apps/desktop`.

### M16 · F-BE-08 decisions
- D-1000: This branch works in `apps/api` only, in parallel with the session that owns `apps/desktop`, `apps/web`, `packages/ui` and `packages/protocol/hosted-features.json`. Where a contract needed a reading, it is written down here and in the pull request rather than changed in a client. Decisions start at D-1000 because the D-900s are taken.
- D-1001: A call's hold grows with the call. The mint holds 10 minutes, as F-BE-08 says, but a call may run 30 minutes, and `ledger.settle` never charges more than is held. So:
  - The hold's reservation expires 10 minutes after the call's cap, so the 15-minute reservation sweeper (F-BE-05) never takes a live call's hold.
  - A doer round within 2 minutes of the time the hold pays for takes up to 10 more minutes from the balance (`ledger.topUp`, which takes what there is, within a Team member's cap). A call that has run past its paid time with nothing left gets 402 `CREDITS_EXHAUSTED` from `/voice/act`, which is the "402 → end" of ARCHITECTURE §12.6.
  - Settling charges the full `ceil(s/10)×20`, taking anything above the hold from the balance as far as it goes; a balance never goes below 0.
- D-1002: Settle holds the app's seconds to the call. They are never more than the time since the mint plus 5 s of clock skew, and never less than the time of the call's last doer round less 15 s to connect (`voice_sessions.last_act_at`). Without a usage report the API can't see connected time; the floor stops an app from claiming 0 s after using the doer.
- D-1003: The doer keeps no state between rounds, so no prompt or answer is stored (F-BE-06).
  - Each round is one Messages API call: the request, the current snapshot and the results so far, fenced as data, with the closed catalog as tools. The tools and the system prompt are cached.
  - The API mints each tool call's id as `vc.<round>.<index>.<tool>.<short arguments>`. The short arguments are the call's ids, numbers and choices in base64url; free text is replaced by its length, so an id never carries what the user said.
  - The next round rebuilds its history and its round number from the ids the app sends back. The app should send the results of every call so far in that request, not only the last round's.
  - Round 8 runs with `tool_choice: none` and has to answer in words.
- D-1004: The doer and Find work default to Claude Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`) at low effort when remote config names no model. They run up to 8 rounds a request, many a minute, inside the 120 credits a minute the call already pays. Remote config's `ai_models.voice_act` and `voice_resolve` still override it. This branch leaves `config/remote-config.json` alone because a Rust test holds it equal to the desktop's bundled copy. `ClaudeAi` sends `output_config.effort` only to models that take it (not Haiku 4.5 or Sonnet 4.5), so an override to one of those still works.
- D-1005: The doer's catalog is F-CN-02's table as 47 typed, closed tools (`services/voiceTools.ts`), written to `apps/api/voice-tools.json` for the desktop's ActionRegistry.
  - Parameter names are this branch's; the spec lists only some.
  - `set_setting` and `get_setting` take the closed list as real `SettingKey`s: `theme`, `notify_system`, `notify_sound`, `dictate_engine`, `dictate_mic`, `pause_all_mail`, `conductor_voice`, `conductor_tone`, `conductor_verbosity`.
  - `set_thread_mode` takes the real `ThreadMode` values `plan` and `act`; the spec's table says plan/agent.
  - The setting keys, settings pages, thread modes, sidebar tabs and isolation choices are checked against the app's generated protocol types (`satisfies`), so a rename in the app fails the API's typecheck instead of a call.
  - Calls outside the catalog or its types never reach the app. When nothing usable is left, the answer is "I can't do that by voice." Forced lane removal, Full access and plugin spend are absent (F-CN-03).
  - The request, snapshot and results pass the server's redactor before the model sees them, on top of the app's own redaction (F-CN-03), and `<` is escaped so data can't close its tag.
- D-1006: Realtime providers sit behind `RealtimeProvider.mint({instructions, voice, maxSeconds})`.
  - `MockRealtime` (dev and tests) returns `ws://<api>/v1/dev/realtime?token=…`.
  - `OpenAiRealtime` uses the vendor's client-secret endpoint (`POST /v1/realtime/client_secrets`, model `gpt-realtime` unless `REALTIME_MODEL` says otherwise). It creates the session with the talker's base instructions, the voice and the one `act` tool; the app adds persona, tone and the workspace summary once connected. The token is good for connecting for 10 minutes.
  - `REALTIME_PROVIDER` and `OPENAI_API_KEY` choose the provider (ARCHITECTURE §17.5; MILESTONES' HUMAN_TODO calls the key `REALTIME_API_KEY`, and the architecture's name wins). Swapping providers through remote config waits for a second vendor and a desktop config update.
  - In production the mock refuses (503 `VOICE_UNAVAILABLE`) rather than hand out a dev socket.
  - The dev WebSocket mock `GET /v1/dev/realtime` is not built here: its protocol is whatever the desktop's MockRealtime speaks, so it belongs with that client.
- D-1007: `expires_at` in the session answer is when the call ends at the latest (`started_at + max_seconds`), the instant `voice_sessions.expires_at` holds. The token's own expiry is the provider's. The voice sweeper settles a call 2 minutes after that: at the provider's reported seconds when it reports usage, else at `max_seconds`, which charges no more than the hold (1,200 credits, or more only if the doer topped it up). OpenAI has no per-session usage report, so an abandoned call pays its hold; the app should keep an open call's id and settle it after a crash.
- D-1008: Find work (`/voice/resolve`) asks for JSON by schema and holds the answer to the offered ids: each once, scores clamped to 0..1, best first, at most 10. An empty candidate list answers `[]` with no model call and no charge. It is gated like the rest of Conductor (plan `conductor` and the Team policy); the app falls back to local fuzzy matching on any failure (F-CN-05). New per-user limits: `voice_act` 60 a minute and `voice_resolve` 30 a minute, next to §14.9's `voice_session` 10 an hour.

### M16 · F-BE-08 log
- Tests first, then the code. On the first full run every voice route test passed. One catalog test failed: 9 tool descriptions (such as "Open a thread.") were too short to tell the model when to use them. The descriptions were rewritten rather than the check relaxed.
- The existing guard that every hosted route is plan-gated (`test/hosted.test.ts`) then failed for the new routes, as it should. It now lists session, act and resolve under `conductor`, and settle as the one route that reaches only the caller's own call (with a test that it can't reach anyone else's).
- The whole API suite passes: 24 files, 180 tests. Gate, after merging the latest `product-m0-m5`: green (Rust 818, desktop 362, UI 358, API 180, web 53, protocol 9; clippy, fmt and the protocol drift check).

## M16 · 16A Conductor core (F-CN-01…04, F-CN-08)
Built on branch `m16a-conductor` (from `product-m0-m5`), in parallel with F-CN-06 and F-CN-07 on `m16a-persona-wake`, whose seams are agreed: that branch's persona and memory reach a call as a `PersonaBrief` read once at start, and its wake detector starts calls through `ConductorService::start(CallOrigin::Wake)`. Decisions are numbered from D-1100.
- [x] F-CN-01 Call lifecycle, F-CN-02 talker/doer and tool catalog, F-CN-03 delivery rules and safety, F-CN-04 HUD, ring and call log, F-CN-08 audio routing (first slice).
  - **`tandem-core::voice_tools`.** The closed catalog: 47 tools, each mapped to registry commands or marked voice-only, with what it needs on screen (`Review`), and the closed `set_setting` list. Tests hold it equal to `apps/api/voice-tools.json` (names, groups, which tools confirm) and check that every mapped command exists (AC1).
  - **`tandem-voice::realtime`.** `RealtimeProvider` gives normalized `ClientEvent`s and `ServerEvent`s. `MockRealtime` is a tokio-tungstenite client for the dev mock's protocol. There is also the 3 s pre-connect buffer (`buffer::PreConnect`) and the PCM16 24 kHz helpers (a streaming resampler and base64).
  - **`tandem-voice::conductor`.**
    - `CallController` handles start, connect, the talker, doer rounds, mute, the cap, end and settle.
    - `Executor` is the local tool executor. A second closed-schema check uses the same JSON, and it enforces the closed list, the confirmations, the waiting- and shell-pane refusals, control-character stripping, redaction and cancellation.
    - `Confirms` holds questions bound to request ids. A yes on a screen-only question needs a `LocalReview`.
    - The rest: the view types for the HUD, `PersonaBrief`, and `Speaker` (cpal, or silent for tests).
  - **Mic (F-CN-08).** `AudioService::begin_stream` streams a call through Dictate's device policy, gain and silent-mic recovery; recovery events go to the call, not to Dictate. While a call holds the mic, Dictate refuses every take, from the global key and from composers ("Conductor is listening").
  - **Desktop.**
    - `services/conductor.rs` holds the service, the hosted-error mapping, the workspace snapshot (names and states only) and the call-log tidy at startup: unsettled calls settle, and logs past 7 days go.
    - `commands/conductor.rs` has the `AppHost` and the commands `conductor_start_call`, `end_call`, `mute`, `confirm` (main window only), `state`, `call_log` and `call_delete`, plus the debug-only `test_conductor_inject` and `test_conductor_clock`.
    - `ApiClient` gains `voice_session`, `voice_act` and `voice_settle`.
    - `tandem-store::repo::conductor` keeps the call log in the existing tables.
    - `type_prompt_with` adds a `send` flag, and `settings::store_and_apply` is the path `set_setting` uses.
    - Sign-out ends a call first, so it can still settle.
  - **UI.**
    - The title-bar ring (UI-01 item 8): Pro only; a click toggles a call, holding it is push-to-talk.
    - The HUD (UI-61): state, utterance, newest 3 chips plus "+N", the question row with "Confirm on screen", Mute, Call log and End, the credits meter, errors with their actions, and the 3 s receipt.
    - The call log sheet (UI-66), with empty, loading, error and populated states.
    - Palette commands `conductor.call` (Mod+Shift+Space; on Free it opens the plans), `conductor.mute` and `conductor.log`.
    - `WaveformRing` gains `paused`: no animation frames while the window is hidden or the app inactive. `ringStats` counts frames.
  - **Mock and harness.** `scripts/mock-realtime.ts` is a dependency-free WebSocket server with a control API. The API takes `REALTIME_MOCK_URL`, and the E2E runner starts the mock and points the API at it.
  - **Tests.**
    - Rust: catalog 4; schema 3; confirmations 3; executor 13; call controller 15 (AC1 65 s = 140 credits, AC2, AC3, AC4, mute, hang-up while connecting, startup errors, chips, voice-answer binding, 402, barge-in, one call at a time, injection); realtime 8; speaker 2; capture 2 (F-CN-08); Dictate 1; store 2; desktop service 3.
    - UI: ring pause 1; HUD, ring, commands and call log 13.
    - API: mock provider 2.
    - Invariants: rule 5's live voice path, 2 new.
    - E2E-M16-1 (`m16-1-conductor.e2e.ts`): all 8 cases.
  - **Not in this slice.**
    - 23 tools answer honestly that they "aren't available by voice in this version yet" (D-1107): launch_agents, wait_for_pane, handoff, the thread tools besides listing, the teammate tools besides listing, the task tools, run_routine, list_routines, get_usage and check_updates. Wiring them to their services is the next slice.
    - Also not built yet: the floating ring window, the "Keep call logs" setting, "talk faster/slower", and the vendor realtime client (D-1113).

### M16 · 16A Conductor core decisions
- D-1100: Conductor's logic lives in `tandem-voice` (`realtime`, `conductor`) behind host traits (`ToolHost`, `CallHost`), so every rule is tested with fakes; the desktop's `AppHost` is the running app. Calls run on a runtime handle the controller keeps, so any thread can start one (the wake detector included).
- D-1101: F-CN-02 AC1 reads "every tool maps to a registry command, or is one of the table's voice-only tools", since half the table (`prompt_pane`, `read_pane`, …) has no palette command and adding one each would put voice-only actions in the palette. The app holds its catalog equal to the API's `voice-tools.json` and checks each call against that file's schemas again before it runs.
- D-1102: The dev mock's protocol is the app's own: JSON text frames, PCM16 24 kHz base64, events `session.update`, `input_audio.append`, `function_call.output`, `response.create` and back `session.ready`, `speech.started`, `transcript.user|assistant`, `audio.delta`, `function_call`, `response.done`, `error`. It is served by `scripts/mock-realtime.ts` (not the API's `/v1/dev/realtime`, which D-1006 left to this branch); the API's `MockRealtime` points there when `REALTIME_MOCK_URL` is set. The token goes in the handshake's Authorization header and is never logged.
- D-1103: The pre-connect buffer keeps the latest 3 s; audio while muted is never buffered, and mute or hang-up empties it.
- D-1104: `prompt_pane` refuses a pane waiting on an approval or a question at once ("waiting for approval", nothing typed) and refuses shell panes; a busy agent gets the prompt once it is back at its prompt, waiting up to 60 s, and checks the call's cancellation right before typing, so a hang-up while it waits delivers nothing (AC2).
- D-1105: A spoken answer is `act("answer <request id> yes|no")`, or a bare yes/no for the question the HUD shows; the app handles it without a doer round and resolves exactly that id. A spoken yes for a shell command is refused; only `conductor_confirm` from the `main` window makes the `LocalReview` that `run_in_shell` takes. The HUD shows one question at a time, oldest first; the talker is told each question's id as it comes up.
- D-1106: A call's id is the API's session id. The call log uses the existing `conductor_calls` and `conductor_call_events` tables, so there is no migration to collide with parallel branches. Connected seconds are saved every 10 s, so a call interrupted by a crash settles at the next start. Settled calls older than 7 days are purged at startup.
- D-1107: Tools not yet wired to an app service return a plain failure ("… isn't available by voice in this version yet; it's in the app.") rather than pretending; the doer tells the user so.
- D-1108: Two debug-only harness commands: `test_conductor_inject` runs doer tool calls the mock doer doesn't plan through the same executor, chips and log; `test_conductor_clock` moves only the call's clock, because `TANDEM_FAKE_CLOCK` would freeze the whole app. The 65 s case runs in real time, so the API's settle clamp (D-1002) holds and the credits are the real ones.
- D-1109: Sign-out ends a call and waits up to 3 s for its settle before the tokens go.
- D-1110: While a call holds the mic, Dictate refuses every take: from the global key and from composers. The owner is set through Dictate's `set_mic_owner` until `m16a-persona-wake`'s `MicOwners` registry lands. The composer mic shows "Conductor is listening".
- D-1111: The persona seam is `PersonaBrief {voice, instructions}`, read once per call; the app's rules and the fenced workspace summary follow it. Built-in defaults until F-CN-06's `CallContext` is mapped onto it.
- D-1112: The desktop's balance doesn't show the 10-minute hold while a call runs (the API sends no credits event on reserve); the HUD's minutes-left reads the balance as shown and the settle corrects it.
- D-1113: Only the mock provider is built on the desktop. A session minted for another provider ends with "Couldn't connect to Conductor" and settles at 0 s; the vendor client waits for the key (HUMAN_TODO).

### M16 · 16A Conductor core log
- Tests first from the eight E2E-M16-1 cases, the four F-CN-02 and two F-CN-03 acceptance criteria. The first E2E run crashed the app: `conductor_start_call` is a sync command and spawned on a thread outside the runtime (D-1100's runtime handle fixes it). Then the test-only commands lacked their capability (added to `test-capability.json`), `getText()` read "" while the HUD faded in (read `innerText`), and a key off the closed list was refused by the schema check with a generic reason; the executor now says "Conductor can't change that setting." first. One unit test raced the call's tick, and was fixed in the test rather than slowed.

## Decisions
- D-1: F-CD-09 owned by M9. It needs the Files editor and the Tandem browser.
- D-2: F-SH-08 framework slice in M5, Accounts step in M6, owned by M8.
- D-3: F-DI-09 owned by M8 so dictionary actions exist before the Enhance interplay.
- D-4: M7 builds the `tandem-gateway` skeleton (loopback MCP, leases, approvals) as a slice of F-PL-01.
- D-5: M7 ships `/v1/ai/title` with a mock provider; the real provider lands in M13.
- D-6: Dashboard approvals are tested on the core ApprovalBroker in M5, again with a real thread in M7.
- D-7: Thread Running → Interrupted is tested on the sessions table in M5, in the UI in M7.
- D-8: "Create with AI" is tested against an API fixture in M10; the real route lands in M13.
- D-9: Threat-model rule tests land beside each mechanism; M15 owns F-TL-06 and audits completeness.
- D-10: Test-only dependencies (wiremock, fast-check, vt100, …), the `sshd` compose profile and the `tandem-invariants` crate don't change the runtime stack.
- D-11: One repository with independent deployables (desktop, API, web, sidecars). The user asked whether to split into several repos; DECISIONS fixes a monorepo, and the generated protocol types tie the desktop, API and web to one commit. Revisit only when a deployable gets its own release cadence.
- D-12 (consistency pass, CONSISTENCY_REPORT "Decisions made in this pass"): composer dictation uses the global Dictate key (⌥Space also on macOS; Alt+Space unbound on Windows/Linux); light accent teal-700 #0F766E; meter thresholds 80/95%; Free = 1 teammate besides Lead; thread modes stored `plan`/`act`, access `read`/`edits`/`full`; one main window; Plan/Act `Mod+Shift+P`, model picker `Mod+Shift+O`; global gate G is the 3-line MILESTONES gate.
- D-13: The earlier session's cloud sync log (`/v1/sync/events`, local `events` table) was removed. It has no feature ID, it isn't in the ARCHITECTURE §14.6 endpoint catalogue, and uploading local events by default would break DECISIONS rule 4.
- D-14: Unit-only Rust enums serialize as plain snake_case strings (`"forbidden_plan"`), not `{type: …}`. ARCHITECTURE §3.1's `tag = "type"` rule is applied to enums that carry data.
- D-15: The whole ARCHITECTURE §4.2 DDL ships as `0001_init.sql` now (MILESTONES M0 lists only four tables). No user data exists yet, and one shipped schema avoids migration churn across milestones.
- D-16: Redaction corpus lines mark each fake secret with «…» and split it with `{{}}` so repository secret scanners don't flag fixtures; the test strips the marker. The benign corpus is 1,248 real lines sampled from Python stdlib, C headers, npm/eslint sources and this repo. Two sampled lines that held a real-format token and a password-bearing URL were removed: they aren't benign.
- D-17: The desktop E2E injects axe-core into the webview with `browser.execute` because `@axe-core/webdriverio` opens helper windows, which a Tauri webview cannot. WebdriverIO runs with `wdio:enforceWebDriverClassic` (WebKitWebDriver has no BiDi).
- D-18: Vite 7 across the desktop app so vitest and the app share one Vite major.
- D-19: `GET /v1/config` sends `payload` as the RFC 8785 canonical JSON *text* and signs its UTF-8 bytes, so the desktop verifies without re-canonicalizing. The bundled fallback is `resources/config.json`, a copy of `apps/api/config/remote-config.json` (a Rust test keeps them equal).
- D-20: API tests run against the compose Postgres and Redis (a fresh `tandem_test` database per run) instead of fakes; `pnpm gate` therefore needs `pnpm infra:up`. CI's test-ts job starts the compose services.
- D-21: Added the dev-only `POST /v1/billing/mock/portal` (declared in ARCHITECTURE §14.6) so the mock portal can cancel at period end through the same webhook handler, and test-only `/__test/user/delete` and `/__test/subscriptions` for re-runnable E2E.
- D-22: The API serves better-auth with `baseURL = WEB_PUBLIC_URL`; browsers reach it through the web app's `/v1/*` rewrite so session cookies are first-party. SSE responses set `Cache-Control: no-cache, no-transform` so the proxy doesn't gzip-buffer the stream.
- D-23: The rate limiter's refill clamps negative elapsed time to zero (found when the test clock moved backwards and locked a client out).
- D-24: Stripe fixture event ids derive from the user, so replays are no-ops (M1 verification); E2E passes a nonce for fresh events.
- D-25: The analytics id lives in its own file, `$DATA/telemetry/analytics-id` (MILESTONES E2E-M2-7 checks "the analytics id file"), instead of a settings row. Opting out deletes the file.
- D-26: Crash reports use a minimal Sentry envelope client over `Net` instead of the `sentry` crate, so every request shows in `TANDEM_NET_LOG` and nothing else can send; the panic hook only writes scrubbed reports under `$DATA/crashes/`.
- D-27: `TANDEM_FAKE_PICK_FOLDER` (debug only, declared in ARCHITECTURE §17.5) makes `workspace_pick_folder` return a path, or the contents of a file it names, because WebDriver can't drive the native dialog. `pane_list` added to §3.2 until `layout_get` lands in M3.
- D-28: ts-rs 12 with `TS_RS_LARGE_INT=number`: JSON over IPC carries i64/u64 as numbers, so the generated TypeScript says `number`.
- D-29: Each PTY IPC call gets its own JS `Channel`: when Rust drops a channel (a failed `pty_attach`) the JS side closes it for good, so reusing it for `pty_spawn` lost every frame.
- D-30: Status badges keep their §1.3 `*-text` colour on the surface with a tinted border instead of a soft tinted fill: the text tokens pass AA on surfaces (UI_SPEC §7.3) but the light amber/green/rose text fell to ~4.2:1 on their own tints. Found by the both-themes axe pass.
- D-31: The E2E keychain is in memory (`TANDEM_FAKE_KEYCHAIN=1`), so a relaunched app is signed out by design; specs that relaunch use "Use Tandem offline" (persisted without secrets) instead of a file-backed fake keychain, which would put secrets on disk.
- D-32: Signing out also leaves offline mode (back to UI-03). The window state is also saved 400 ms after each change so a crash or kill keeps it, and a restored window that isn't fully on one display is re-centred (the plugin alone only checks overlap).
- D-33: Panes render into host elements the grid moves between layout slots (`paneHosts.ts`), so swap, move and maximize never remount xterm: the WebGL context and scrollback stay (F-CD-02 AC2, AC3).
- D-34: A fresh tab starts a Shell (E2E-M2-1 expects "a tab with a live shell"); a tab whose last pane closed, or that replaced the last closed tab, shows UI-10's launch tiles instead.
- D-35: `pane_move {paneId, tabId}` (declared in ARCHITECTURE §3.2) moves a pane to another tab of its workspace with the same process: the tab-strip drop and "Move to new tab".
- D-36: The command registry carries the three binding columns of UI_SPEC §5 (`mac`, `pc`, `pcTerminal`); titles are `cmd.<id>`. It lists only commands that have a handler and grows each milestone; a test checks every `pcTerminal` binding against the terminal intercept list.
- D-37: Code mode's rail order is `workspaces.sort_key` / `pinned` (`rail_move` with `mode: "code"`); `rail_items` holds the Agent and Thread rails when they land. `rail_width` defaults to 248 within 180–360.
- D-38: Terminal queries are answered in Rust for every pane, visible or hidden, and xterm's own replies are switched off with parser hooks (ARCHITECTURE §5.7). MILESTONES M3 describes xterm answering while visible; ARCHITECTURE outranks it, and both give exactly one reply. CPR uses the vt100 shadow cursor as of the query.
- D-39: Orphans are found with `sysinfo`: a record matches when its PID is alive with the same start time, and on Unix any process in the pane's session (the session id is the recorded PID) also counts, so a `nohup` child of a shell that died with the app is found. Windows relies on the Job Object.
- D-40: `scripts/gate.sh` runs one command per line. Under `set -e` a failure inside an `a && b` list doesn't stop the script, so a red `pnpm -r test` once let the gate exit 0 (found when Postgres was down after a container restart; the earlier gated commits had run every step).
- D-41: The E2E axe helper waits for finite animations to finish before running, so a dialog mid-fade isn't measured as low contrast.
- D-42: A minimal engine registry lands in M3 (`crates/tandem-engines/registry.json`: id, label, vendor, commands, provider home variable, resume, newline sequence) with PATH-only detection behind `engines_list`, because E2E-M3-5 runs an agent pane. M4 adds the rest of §6.1, the detection report and install commands; `EngineView` keeps its shape.
- D-43: The handback mark (`OSC 7770;handback;<token>;<code>`) carries a per-launch token passed as `TANDEM_SHELL_TOKEN`; the wrapper copies it into an unexported shell variable and unsets it before the engine starts, so it's in neither argv (`ps`) nor the engine's environment (§5.8's rule for shell marks).
- D-44: Engines resolve on the login PATH, then the app's own PATH: Linux login profiles reset PATH (`/etc/profile`), which would hide installs the app can see. The working directory is never searched.
- D-45: Each run records its children in `run/children-<app pid>.json` stamped with the app's own PID and start time, and deletes it on a clean exit. The startup scan runs in the background, collects only files whose owner has exited and waits up to 8 s for one still shutting down, so a quick relaunch doesn't report children the old instance is stopping, and two overlapping instances don't overwrite each other's records (both were seen in E2E). `orphans_list` waits for the scan.
- D-46: Find highlights use two new terminal theme tokens (`searchMatch`, `searchActive`, `#RRGGBB` as the search addon requires) so the bar adds no ad-hoc colours. A first search starts from the top of the scrollback, so the first match reads "1 of n" (F-CD-08 AC1).
- D-47: `terminalHandles` and the WebGL pool only drop an entry its owner registered, so a late unmount of an earlier view can't remove the live one.
- D-48: E2E specs share one `TANDEM_HOME`, so each launch restores the previous spec's workspace. The reset removes workspaces through IPC and then reloads the webview, so the UI never shows a pane the backend has closed; failures also save `__tandemTest.debug()` (stores, panes, focus, dialogs) next to the screenshot.
- D-49: Shell-integration marks are filtered out of the PTY stream in Rust before the ring, the side channel and the view see them (`MarkFilter`, split-safe across reads).
