Valid OSC 133 marks go back into the stream without the token, so the view can decorate commands while the token never reaches the webview, the ring or snapshots; marks without the token are dropped. "Copy last command output" is captured as plain text between C and D by the side channel (vte-stripped, `\r` and `ESC[K` applied) rather than from ring offsets, which the ring's eviction would invalidate. ARCHITECTURE §5.8 updated.
- D-50: bash runs `--rcfile <shim> -i` with login emulation (`/etc/profile`, then the first of `~/.bash_profile`, `~/.bash_login`, `~/.profile`); zsh files are sourced at the top level of the shim (never inside a function, so `typeset` stays global) and ZDOTDIR goes back to the user's value when startup ends; fish leaves the user's prompt alone, so it has no B mark; PowerShell writes C before PSReadLine echoes the line break and tags it `nl=1` so that break isn't captured. Agent wrappers never get the integration environment (a zsh wrapper would load the shim and take the token); the shell they hand back to does.
- D-51: A plain shell's status dot follows its marks: working between C and D, idle once nothing has printed for 2 s after D. Agent status arrives with M4.
- D-52: Under a full E2E run, WebKitWebDriver occasionally lost the click on a picker tile right after the dialog opened (3/3 isolated runs and an event trace showed normal delivery). The split helper waits for animations, then clicks once more only if the picker is still open and its tiles are enabled (they are disabled while a pick runs, so the retry can't launch twice). E2E-M3-1 builds its 4×4 grid over IPC (`pane_create`, `layout_save`, reload): splitting stops at the 240×120 minimum before 16 panes fit the test window, and the spec checks rendering, not splitting.
- D-53: A paste into a program with bracketed paste on sends its text between the markers with line breaks as LF (F-CD-05 AC2 spells `ESC[200~a\nb ESC[201~`) and removes any marker inside the text, so pasted text can't end the bracket early and run the rest. Without bracketed paste, line breaks are typed as CR, as terminals do.
- D-54: The view measures its character cell again once it is in the document and whenever the font set finishes loading, and every refit goes through the PTY resize; a resize sent before the spawn lands is forgotten and reconciled once connected. Found by E2E-M3-2: xterm measured a fallback font in the detached pane host, so the PTY kept 42 rows while the view showed 36 and TUIs drew off-screen. E2E-M3-2 now checks `stty size` against the view.
- D-55: PTY resizes wait for the layout to settle (80 ms with no size change) after a divider drag or becoming visible, then throttle at 50 ms. Panel groups land over a few frames after pointer-up, which gave some panes two resizes (E2E-M3-3 asserts one per affected PTY and none elsewhere).
- D-56: TUI goldens are keyed by terminal size (`apps/desktop/e2e/goldens/<name>.<cols>x<rows>.json`, from the serialize addon), written with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` and reviewed by hand; htop keeps only its column header and key bar (its numbers move). E2E-M3-1 and E2E-M3-3 put a Pro user in place through the API's `/__test/plan` route and build their grids over IPC.
- D-57: Output pacing (F-CD-04): the focused pane writes at once; every other pane's output waits in one scheduler that hands xterm at most 256 KB per 16 ms tick, round-robin, only after the previous tick has parsed, and visits each pane at most every 33 ms. Unparsed output isn't acked, so the Rust ack window holds the programs back. Timers, not frames, so hidden panes keep draining. The perf suite runs a `--profile perf` binary (release with debug assertions).
- D-58: The WebGL pool batches every visibility change of one task and attaches one context per frame. Panes shown together tie and keep the contexts they hold, so a mode switch back to 16 panes attaches nothing (it used to evict and re-attach 8 contexts synchronously: 1.2 s). "GPU rendering: Off" never applied: the setting stores `"off"` and the view compared with `false`; it now resets the pool.
- D-59: New terminal views open one per frame, focused first (`mountQueue`); one view takes 60–100 ms here. The cell is measured again only when the web font was still loading at open — a re-measure costs a full re-render (200–300 ms) and every new pane did one, twice. A font face that loads later (bold, italic) clears the WebGL glyph atlas instead.
- D-60: Pane `LANG` defaults to `C.UTF-8` on Linux (`en_US.UTF-8` elsewhere) when it's unset or empty; `en_US.UTF-8` isn't installed on many Linux systems, and programs then fall back to ASCII.
- D-61: zsh breaks its own command line with real newlines at the pane width (program output wraps softly and `paneText` joins it). E2E assertions about typed command lines join lines (`waitForPaneText(…, { commandLine: true })`).
- D-62: Remote engine entries come from the config's `registry_overrides` (ARCHITECTURE §14.10), in the registry's camelCase field names.
- D-63: The detection probe runs the login shell with the captured login environment (not the app's), so what it finds is what a terminal finds. Debug builds read `TANDEM_DETECT_SYSTEM_DIRS`, `TANDEM_DETECT_NO_NPM` and `TANDEM_DETECT_DEADLINE_SECS` so tests don't see engines installed on the machine.
- D-64: Cargo can't set `panic = "abort"` per package; `tandem-hook` has no panicking paths, ignores every error and always exits 0.
- D-65: An engine's default account is checked and inserted in one write, so agents launched together share it (six at once raced into the unique index); provider-home files go through unique temp names.
- D-66: Added `status_list` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2) for the rail and pane headers after a webview reload; `status:changed` also lists `removed` keys when an agent leaves its pane. The full Dashboard snapshot (`status_snapshot`) stays with M5.
- D-67: F-CD-11's auto-arrange (⌈√n⌉ columns, row-major) backs the Workspace menu item and Mod+Shift+T; PRODUCT_SPEC wins over UI_SPEC's preset table where they differ (3 panes: 2+1, not 1+2).
- D-68: `tandem-hook` is looked up next to the app binary (`TANDEM_HOOK_BIN` in debug builds). Without it, panes get status from heuristics only. Bundling it as a Tauri sidecar is part of M5's release pipeline.
- D-69: Hook-reported status wins for a pane from its first verified report; heuristics stay quiet for it from then on.
- D-70: The E2E app runs with a desktop-session environment (HOME, DISPLAY, XDG_*, TANDEM_*, PLAYWRIGHT_*, …) and a minimal PATH, not the test runner's. Panes inherit the app's environment, and the runner's CI/agent variables showed up as token-shaped values in the spawn log (E2E-M4-4); engines installed on the build machine also leaked into detection.
- D-71: Check again captures the login environment again but keeps the previous capture when the new one is degraded (a shell that's slow right now doesn't lose a good PATH).
- D-72: Added `engines_report` (the cached last report) so the launch grid's diagnostics survive a webview reload, and the test-only `test_account_create` (a second engine account with its own home; the sign-in flow is F-AC-02 in M6) and API `POST /__test/config` (re-signs the remote config with new `registry_overrides`).
- D-73: `PaneRecord` carries `accountId`; `pane_create {accountId}` pins an agent pane to an account of its own engine, otherwise the engine's default account.
- D-74: Where UI_SPEC and PRODUCT_SPEC/MILESTONES differ on the sidebar, the latter win: it floats below 1,100 px (not 1,280) and its tabs are closable, reorderable pills. The close confirmation uses PRODUCT_SPEC's title ("Stop {name}? It's working.") with UI_SPEC's body and buttons.
- D-75: An agent pane's first name sits beside its title instead of replacing it; the title chain (F-EN-06) is unchanged. Names are drawn at random from the free ones inside the pane's write transaction, so panes created together can't collide; when all 200 are in use a number is added ("Ada 2").
- D-76: `SourceRef` gains a test-only `fixture` variant (E2E fixture agents). The `approvals` table doesn't record fixture sources (its CHECK allows thread, teammate_chat and gateway).
- D-77: `ui:focus` carries the pane's `{workspaceId, tabId}` so a card or notification can open a workspace whose layout hasn't loaded yet.
- D-78: A real system notification's click isn't routed to its source yet: the notification plugin has no click callback on desktop. Clicking one brings Tandem forward on macOS and most Linux desktops; the jump to the source is covered through the recording notifier (`test_notifier_click`) and the bell. HUMAN_TODO lists the manual check; wiring the click per OS goes with M15's hardening.
- D-79: `notify_types` keys are flat, `"<type>.<channel>"` (`"done.system": false`): settings maps hold scalar values only.
- D-80: Notification paging uses `"<createdAt>:<seq>"` (SQLite rowid) so items created in the same millisecond keep their order and none is skipped.
- D-81: The approval broker makes its expiry timer inside the spawned task and needs a runtime only when one exists: a request opened from a sync command once crashed the app ("no reactor running").
- D-82: The E2E axe check waits for the theme to land, colours to stop changing and every open dialog to be fully opaque; WebKit doesn't list every fade in `getAnimations()`, and a half-faded sheet read as low contrast (E2E-M2-2 failed intermittently under load). Failures now print the measured colours and ratio.
- D-83: `restore_get_snapshot` returns only what restore adds (paused cards with `canResume`/`reason`, interrupted threads, the auto-resume flag); workspaces, tabs, layouts and the sidebar keep loading through their own commands, and `PaneRecord` carries `restoreState`. `restore_resume` / `restore_start_fresh` take `{paneId, cols?, rows?}` and start the PTY with no view; the pane's view then attaches with `pty_attach` and replays. Clicks and auto-resume share that one path. The saved scrollback shows (dimmed, ending in "— Restored output —") in a read-only view under the card; once resumed, the live view starts with the new process's output rather than prepending the old (§5.6 asks for the same xterm; revisit with the Files/terminal work in M9).
- D-84: Auto-resume is started by the UI once the app shell shows (`restore_auto_resume`, once per launch), so agents never run behind the sign-in screen. An agent holds one of the two queue slots from launch until it is ready (§6.5) or 10 s pass.
- D-85: Each agent session stores its launch configuration as JSON (`{extraArgs, home}`) and Resume uses it, never the current settings. A configuration that no longer parses, or a provider home that's gone, reads "Launch configuration unavailable" and only Start fresh is offered.
- D-86: Engine session ids from hooks are written to the running session row as soon as they are reported, so a kill -9 can still resume. The fake engine reports `fake-<pid>` and honours `--resume <id>` ("Resumed session <id>").
- D-87: Quit stops every PTY tree in parallel (SIGHUP → SIGTERM → SIGKILL, 2 s apart) with a 5 s deadline, after which whatever is left is force-killed. The menu's Quit stores pending layout saves first.
- D-88: Kill-fuzz (`scripts/killfuzz.ts`) kill -9s the real app at random points (during startup, or with its shells live) and the store's own writer mid-transaction, then checks integrity and foreign keys, layouts against open panes, paused agents, orphans and crash reports. `TANDEM_SNAPSHOT_EVERY_MS` (debug builds) shortens the 5-minute scrollback snapshot for it and the E2E.
- D-89: The splash says "Restoring your workspace…" (UI_SPEC copy; MILESTONES says "session"), then "Checking your plan…", bounded to 3 s. Any key or click skips the wait. It only fades in (no other motion), and with Reduce Motion it appears at once.
- D-90: Threads arrive in M7, so E2E-M5-3 seeds a bare `threads` row through `test_thread_seed` and reads it back with `test_thread_state` (D-7).
- D-91: The agent session row is written before the engine process starts (and dropped if the spawn fails): with four agents starting at once a SessionStart hook could beat the insert, and the session id Resume needs was lost (E2E-M5-2 resumed 3 of 4).
- D-92: The child registry writes its file while holding its lock. Two shells starting in the same millisecond could leave an older list on disk, so a crash at that moment would hide an orphan from the next scan (found by kill-fuzz, reproduced by `f_sh_07_concurrent_spawns_all_reach_the_registry_file`).
- D-93: Updates sit behind an `UpdateBackend` trait (Tauri's updater in the app, a fake in unit tests) so the state machine (stages, anti-rollback, min-version grace, channel, deb) is tested without a window. A failed check never shows the pill (only download, verify and install failures do); "Check now" waits for a check already running.
- D-94: Release builds only trust `TAURI_SIGNING_PUBLIC_KEY` compiled in from CI; debug builds trust the dev key `uk-1` (`pnpm keys:dev`, public half in `resources/keys.json`). Plain-HTTP feeds are allowed only in debug builds and only on 127.0.0.1 (the plugin's config is patched at startup). Under the E2E harness the install step records the version in `$DATA/test-update-installed` instead of replacing the running debug binary, and the app doesn't restart; check, download and minisign verification are the real plugin's.
- D-95: The minimum-version notice starts when a response first names a higher minimum (persisted, so relaunching doesn't reset the 72 h); a lower minimum clears it. The banner sits first in the UI-133 priority order. The modal overlay is re-read on focus and every minute; E2E moves the notice into the past (`test_min_version_shift`) instead of mocking the whole clock. The API's test routes can now set `min_app_version`.
- D-96: The release workflow builds Linux on ubuntu-22.04, not in a `debian:bullseye` container (ARCHITECTURE §17.4 updated): Tauri 2 needs webkit2gtk-4.1, which bullseye doesn't ship. Notices and SBOMs are generated once in their own job and every platform build embeds the same notices.
- D-97: npm licences and the npm SBOM come from `pnpm --filter "desktop..." licenses list --prod` (the app plus its workspace packages, production only). `license-checker` and `@cyclonedx/cyclonedx-npm` read `npm ls`, which under pnpm reports missing dependencies and lists dev packages (2,089 components instead of 106), so `scripts/gen-sbom-npm.ts` writes the CycloneDX 1.5 file itself. Rust uses `cargo-about` (`about.toml`) and `cargo-cyclonedx`.
- D-98: About › Licenses reads notices embedded at build time (build.rs copies `resources/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` into `OUT_DIR`, else a placeholder). It isn't a bundle resource, so dev builds without the generated file still build. The release script and workflow generate it with `--resources` before building.
- D-99: `crates/tandem-voice/models/catalog.json` is the model catalog and the attribution source; its sha256 values stay `TBD` until `scripts/pin-models.ts` (M12), so `release.yml`'s verify step refuses real tags until then and `release-local.sh --dry-run` only warns.
- D-100: The onboarding wizard (UI-04) is, for this slice, the MILESTONES list: Welcome (the existing sign-in screen, UI-03), Engines, Privacy, First workspace. UI-04's theme step and F-SH-08's Accounts and Dictate steps join `STEPS` with their milestones (Accounts M6, Dictate M12; theme is already in Settings). Installs and sign-ins from the Engines step stay in Settings › Engines until the install sheet can run without a workspace. The E2E reset marks the wizard done; `freshProfile()` starts it, and `signInDesktop` skips it on a brand-new profile.
- D-101: The startup perf suite relaunches five times signed in (16 live shells need a paid plan), so `e2e/run.ts --suite startup` runs the real Secret Service keychain: a private session bus and an unlocked gnome-keyring inside the run's HOME (`apt-get install gnome-keyring`). Cold start is measured from the app process's start time (/proc) to the 16-pane layout with the focused shell's prompt. Results on the build VM are in `perf/results/m5.json`; the misses are [manual] for the reference machines (HUMAN_TODO).
- D-102: The public site (an F-WB-01 slice, pulled ahead of M15 so the product has a home at vibezeo.com) is a static export of `apps/web`. Public pages use the `.site.tsx` suffix; `SITE_EXPORT=1` (`pnpm --filter web build:site`) builds only those, with `output: "export"` and trailing slashes, into `apps/web/out`, hosted on Vercel (`apps/web/vercel.json`: Next.js preset with the export, response headers, and a skip rule so desktop-only commits don't redeploy). Vercel's Next.js builder is used even with `framework: null` once `next` is a dependency, so the config doesn't set an output directory (checked with `vercel build`). The normal build serves both the public pages and the portal, so nothing moves when M15 adds pricing, downloads and docs. The site states only what M0–M5 shipped, loads nothing but its own first-party files: no cookies, analytics or third-party resources (F-WB-01 AC2), and its contact, founder and optional legal details (registered name, CIN, address, city: shown only when set) live in `apps/web/lib/site.ts`. The About page follows the usual small-company layout: company, founder, how we work, company details, contact. Server pages import `@tandem/ui` components by path (`@tandem/ui/components/Badge`), because the barrel also exports hook-based client components. Deploy steps: `docs/DEPLOY_SITE.md`.
- D-103: Tandem's hosted AI features run on the Claude API (first-party), not OpenRouter: thread titles, Enhance/Polish rewrites, "Create with AI" teammate drafts, the Auto supervisor and the Conductor's doer (F-BE-06, M13). `AiProvider` gets a `ClaudeAi` implementation selected by `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (`AI_PROVIDER=claude`), with per-feature model ids still in remote config and `MockAi` when the key is absent. Speech-to-text and the realtime voice stay as planned (the Claude API offers neither). The public site lists these as planned, not built (ARCHITECTURE §14, MILESTONES M13).
- D-104: Hardened git reads (TL-06 rule 6) differ from ARCHITECTURE §7's flag list in two ways, both found by the rule-6 test, which first proves its traps fire with plain git. (1) `-c diff.external=` is dropped: in git 2.43 an empty external diff (or `diff.<driver>.command`) makes `git diff` try to run "" and fail, so diff-family reads (`diff`, `log`, `show`, `whatchanged`) get `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` instead, and `blame` gets `--no-textconv`. (2) Filter drivers defined in the repository's own config (`filter.<d>.clean/smudge/process`, local and worktree level) are overridden with empty commands for every CLI read, since `git status` and `git diff` run clean filters; the user's global drivers (git-lfs) still run. Reads that need no CLI (status, diff vs base, head, branches, ancestry, conflicts) go through libgit2, which runs no helpers at all. Lanes are created under the main checkout's root even from a lane, and removing a lane keeps its branch.
- D-105: Early-access builds ship before the hosted service exists. They're compiled with `TANDEM_HOSTED=0` (debug builds also take `TANDEM_HOSTED` or `--test-no-hosted` at run time). `NoHostedApi` answers every API call as offline without a network request; the welcome screen (UI-03) offers only "Start using Tandem" (Free limits, local); `auth_sign_in` returns Unavailable; the 14-day offline deadline (PRODUCT_SPEC §F-SH-08 step 1) doesn't apply; Account & Plan and the plans sheet say Pro and Team open at launch and can't start a checkout. Release builds now default to `api.vibezeo.com` / `app.vibezeo.com` (`tandem.dev` isn't ours) and the bundle id is `com.vibezeo.tandem`; in-app links go to vibezeo.com. The Release workflow's `early-access` channel builds every OS with `TANDEM_HOSTED=0`, ad-hoc signs macOS when there's no Developer ID (Apple Silicon won't run unsigned arm64 code), and publishes a prerelease to the public `NamanJain-Engineer/tandem-releases` (secret `RELEASES_TOKEN`); the voice-model hash check applies once the app depends on `tandem-voice`. E2E: `ea-1-early-access`.
- D-106: A slice of F-BE-06 is pulled ahead of M13 so Tandem's hosted AI runs on the Claude API now: `POST /v1/ai/title` (free, 60/h) and `POST /v1/ai/rewrite` (Enhance/Polish, Pro, 3 credits, 300/day) on `ClaudeAi` (Messages API, 30 s, one retry on 5xx/529, errors carry the status only) when `AI_PROVIDER=claude` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, else `MockAi`. Models come from remote config `ai_models` (Claude Haiku 4.5 for both). User text goes to the model between `<text>` tags under a system prompt that calls it data (TL-06 rule 8). The ledger gains `reserve` / `settle` / `refund` (ARCHITECTURE §14.5; F-BE-05 AC1 concurrency test), a failed call refunds the credits and the daily count, and `ai_requests` keeps metadata only (F-BE-06 AC2 log-capture test). The desktop callers arrive with Thread mode (titles, M7) and Dictate (Enhance/Polish, M8); teammate drafts, Auto and voice stay in M13. Pricing note: at Haiku 4.5 list prices a full 5,000-character Enhance can cost more than its 3 credits; revisit before launch (HUMAN_TODO).
- D-107: Worktree details F-CD-15 leaves open. (1) `lane_remove` without `force` checks for uncommitted or untracked files before it stops the worktree's agents, so a refusal costs nothing (ARCHITECTURE §7 lists the stop first). (2) A worktree created from a worktree starts at that worktree's HEAD and is registered under the repository's top-level workspace: the rail nests one level. (3) An empty slug source gives `tandem/session` (UI-12's fallback), not "lane", which is an internal word (UI_SPEC §6.2). (4) "Create pull request" types `gh pr create` into a new shell pane once the shell has been quiet for 1.5 s after its first output; shell-integration prompt marks aren't kept as pane state, and the quiet rule is the spec's alternative. (5) Removing a repository's workspace now stops its worktrees' panes too (their rows were already soft-removed with it).
- D-108: Launch sheet details F-CD-14 leaves open. (1) Built-in presets live in code with fixed ids `pst_builtin_{solo,pair,bench,fleet}` and can't be overwritten; Fleet is four agents (Builder, Builder, Tester, Reviewer) with a worktree each. (2) Seats that don't get a worktree open together in a new tab named after the task's first words ("Agents" without one), so a launch never rearranges the tab you were in. (3) Browser seats are refused with "Browser panes come in a later version" until the browser pane exists; the sheet doesn't offer them. (4) The Account column joins the lineup with F-AC-02 (task 5); until then every agent uses its engine's default account. (5) An agent's first prompt is the worktree briefing (when it has one), then "Your role: {role}. {brief}" and "Task: {task}", typed as one bracketed paste after the terminal is live (90 s at most); without a task nothing is typed. (6) Without a task, each worktree is named after its agent's role.
- D-109: UI_SPEC's z-index scale puts popovers (100) under sheets (200) and dialogs (300), so a select, menu or tooltip opened in a sheet or dialog was drawn behind its overlay and couldn't be clicked (found by E2E-M6-3). `--z-popover` is now 350: above sheets and dialogs, below toasts (400). A test in `theme.test.tsx` keeps that order.
- D-110: Handoff details F-CD-16 leaves open. (1) UI-13 writes the summary when the dialog opens and lets the user edit it, so `handoff_start` became `handoff_brief` + `handoff_start` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2); the spec's optional extra instruction is the editable summary, a teammate target waits for Teammates, and Thread targets for M7. (2) The source is asked when it's a live agent pane of an engine with a chat adapter (Claude Code, Codex) whose screen doesn't match the engine's `limit_patterns`. The request asks for the brief between `--- handoff brief <nonce> ---` and `--- end of brief <nonce> ---`; a reply counts when at least four of the six headings stand on their own lines, and an empty or malformed reply moves on at once instead of waiting out the 120 s. (3) The registry gains `headless_cmd` (Claude Code `-p --strict-mcp-config --model {model}`, Codex `exec --skip-git-repo-check -c mcp_servers={} --model {model} -`, `{model}` = `cheap_model`), run with the prompt on stdin and exactly the env a pane gets (sanitized login env plus the account's home); the session is fenced as data in the prompt. Debug builds also pass `TANDEM_FAKE_*` so the E2E fake engines can be steered. (4) The note on the source pane lives for the session, not in the database. (5) The target uses its engine's default account until the account picker arrives with F-AC-02/03.
- D-111: Engine-account details F-AC-02/03 leave open. (1) The sign-in is a PTY that isn't a pane: `accounts_begin_add {engineId, label}` returns `LoginSession {opId, paneId, accountId, action, reports}`, the sheet attaches to `paneId` with a small xterm (MiniTerminal), and the end arrives as `accounts:login-finished` (the event ARCHITECTURE already named, with a `state`). Sign in again and Sign out reuse it through `accounts_begin_session`; engines that can't report their sign-in (no `signed_in_file` or `status_cmd`) show "I've signed in" instead (`accounts_finish_login`). (2) The registry gains `signed_in_file` (Claude Code: `.credentials.json`, checked for presence only). The login runs as `exec <engine> [login_cmd]` under the user's login shell, in the account's home; a slash command (`/login`, `/logout`) is typed once a ready pattern shows, after 30 s at most. (3) An agent pane is pinned to its account when it's created (explicit pick, else the workspace override, where a worktree falls back to its repository's, else the engine default), so Make default changes new panes only and a restarted pane keeps its account. The teammate preference joins with Teammates; the resolver already takes it. (4) The AccountChip's choice is the workspace override for that engine (the repository's for a worktree); "Use the default" clears it, and a running pane never switches. (5) Remove deletes the home only when it is exactly `<providers>/<engine>/<id>`, frees the nickname, makes the engine's oldest remaining account the default and drops overrides that named it. Not in this slice: "Open provider folder", the override Edit dialog (the chip sets, × removes) and usage meters (F-AC-04, task 8).
- D-112: Limit details F-AC-05 leaves open. (1) Terminal limits come from the screen checks the status service already makes (at most every 300 ms per pane) plus a trailing check on its 1 s tick, so the last lines of a burst count; a line is reported once, and the same line still on screen after Resume or Stop isn't reported again. The reset parser reads an epoch after `|`, a delay ("in 2h 3m", "in 45 minutes", "in 30s") or a time of day in the user's zone ("resets 4pm", "at 16:12"; tomorrow when already past). (2) In a Code pane UI-36 is a banner under the header, and Choose account… opens the dialog; continuing elsewhere uses the brief fallback (the last 200 redacted lines under the brief's headings) through `limit_continue`, split right of the source. No OS notification on resume yet; threads and `kind` come with M7. (3) E2E-M6-7 uses a reset seconds away on the real clock, because the E2E app runs on the system clock (the status heuristics are time-based); Resume at reset under the FakeClock is unit-tested (`f_ac_05_2_resume_at_reset_acts_at_the_reset_time_and_not_after_a_cancel`). (4) The AccountChip reloads the account list once when its pane's account isn't in it (a default account created with the pane).
- D-113: Usage and context details F-AC-04/06 leave open. (1) Only Codex reports usage to other apps today: `usage_get`/`usage_refresh` start a short-lived `codex app-server` in the account's provider home (its login env, `env_clear`) and read `account/rateLimits/read` (primary/secondary → "5h"/"week", 15 s cap); Claude Code and other engines are `unavailable` with the reason in the chip's tooltip ("Claude Code doesn't report usage to other apps."). Stream rate-limit data (M7) and the local-log estimate (`estimated`, F-AC-07, M16) plug into the same `UsageView`. (2) The 5-minute and after-turn refreshes are driven by the webview (an agent pane's turn ending refreshes its account, at most every 30 s per account), and an account with no reading is probed when the chip first shows it; readings live in memory (`usage_snapshots` stays unused until the dashboard). (3) With no agent pane focused the chip follows the default engine's default account (UI_SPEC §3.2); the spec's "hidden if the engine doesn't report usage" yields to F-AC-04 AC2, which shows "Usage unavailable". (4) Context fill comes from the Stop hook's `transcript_path`, read only when it resolves inside the pane's provider home (links out are refused), from the file's last 256 KB: the last `usage` object's input + cache-read + cache-creation tokens, with no message content kept; the window is 200K, or 1M when the model id says so. Codex terminals have no transcript hook yet, so they show no meter. (5) The chip's two xs meters show from 1536 px, not UI_SPEC's 1280: between the two the title bar overflowed and clipped the window controls (seen in E2E-M6-8's screenshot). The omnibox label truncates instead of wrapping. A reset that isn't today names the day ("Sun 9:00 AM", or "Oct 20, 9:00 AM" beyond six days), in the limit card too. (6) The probe creates the account's provider home when a pane's launch hasn't yet (the chip asks as soon as the pane appears).
- D-114: Onboarding Accounts step (F-SH-08 step 3, MILESTONES M6 exit). The wizard is now Engines, Accounts, Privacy, First workspace; Welcome is the sign-in screen, so AC2's "step 3" is Accounts, and E2E-M5-9 quits and resumes there. The step lists installed engines with a chat adapter whose accounts Vibezeo can keep apart (a home strategy other than `none`), so Cursor Agent, which signs in globally, isn't offered. A signed-in default account reads "Signed in". Otherwise "Sign in to {engine}" opens the Settings › Accounts sheet: Sign in again for the engine's default account, or Add account with the nickname "Personal" filled in when the engine has none. Skip and Continue both move on; nothing is required.
- D-115: Thread event details F-TH-03 leaves open. (1) Text streams as blocks: deltas carry their block's `seq`, and the block closes when the adapter sends its own final text, when any other event arrives, or at `turn_end`; its final text then replaces the partial row (saved every 2 s) in place. So a turn's rows are final at block granularity, not only at `turn_end` (§6.9 said "at turn end"). (2) A subscriber gets the running turn with the open block as an `assistant` event of its text so far, then live deltas; the reducer replaces by `seq` and appends deltas to that item, so a resync after falling behind never doubles text. (3) Stop: a turn the engine reports as an error after a stop is `cancelled`; an acknowledged stop the engine doesn't end within 3 s is ended `cancelled` by the app. A queued follow-up never runs after a stop: it goes back to the thread's draft. (4) The tree kill comes at 3.4 s, so the whole stop is over by AC2's 3.5 s. (5) Windows has no process-group signals: there the stop is the engine's interrupt, then ending the engine process; a Job Object for the whole tree comes with the adapters (M7 tasks 3–4). (6) `thread_events` returns `ThreadEventPage {items, nextBeforeId}`; `thread_subscribe` returns nothing and streams until the webview drops the channel; `threads:changed` and `thread_list` carry the thread rows (there is no separate `ThreadSummary`). (7) A thread without a folder starts in `$DATA/scratch/<thread id>` with Read access; one in a workspace starts with Edits. Remembered per-engine defaults replace both with the composer (M7 task 8).
- D-116: Gateway and Claude adapter details F-PL-01/F-TH-04 leave open. (1) The gateway starts on the first thread session, not at app start (§1 table says "main at startup"): it costs nothing until Thread mode is used. (2) One lease per thread session: a new session (restart, resume) revokes the previous lease; the MCP config is rewritten at `$DATA/mcp/<thread>.json` with mode 0600 (the bearer is a Vibezeo lease, never a vendor token). (3) Origin: absent, `null` or `http://127.0.0.1|localhost:*`; Host: loopback names only (rmcp's checks, 403/400). (4) A bare limit line in an assistant message ("…usage limit reached|<epoch>") isn't shown as text: the result's limit card says it, without the epoch. (5) Context fill on `usage` is a percentage with one decimal against 200K, or 1M when the model id says so. (6) A tool's result row keeps the input detail, since the view replaces the running row with it. (7) Changing the model mid-thread is a new (resumed) session with `--model`; the adapter's `set_model` does nothing for Claude. (8) Approvals offer Once and Thread scopes; "Allow for this thread" rules and inline cards are M7 task 6, until then the Dashboard card answers. The thread is set Waiting before the card exists, so no view shows a card on a Working thread.
- D-118: Thread titles from the hosted AI are opt-in. F-TH-15 asks for `/v1/ai/title` after the first reply, but that sends the first message off the machine, and CLAUDE.md and the product principle ("Nothing leaves the machine by default") forbid that without an opt-in. So every thread gets the local title at once (the first 8 words of its first message, `title_source = fallback`), and the AI title replaces it only when Privacy › "Name threads with AI" (`ai_thread_titles`, default off) is on and the user is signed in; a user rename is never replaced. HUMAN_TODO asks the owner whether to make it default-on, with a consent step, instead.
- D-117: Codex and access details F-TH-05/09 leave open. (1) Only the V2 thread/turn app-server protocol is spoken: Codex versions with only the legacy conversation API are below the registry's `>=0.40.0` and fail closed (the version check uses the detected version, else `initialize`'s `userAgent`; unknown fails closed). (2) The protocol names follow the vendor's documented app-server API and are frozen in fixtures; checking them against a real install is in HUMAN_TODO, since no Codex is available to the build. (3) The gateway lease goes to Codex through `bearer_token_env_var`, so it's in the process env, never in argv or a file; Codex sessions don't get the approvals tool (they ask in-band). (4) In-band approvals: the adapter raises `AdapterEvent::Approval` with its own id and an `ApprovalSubject`; the service shows the same card as for Claude and answers through `ChatSession::resolve_approval`. (5) `thread/tokenUsage/updated`'s `last` request gives `usage`; context fill is its input tokens against `modelContextWindow`. (6) "Reconnecting…" is a note in the turn that restarts an engine process that went away on its own; an idle exit fails nothing. (7) Access is resolved once, in `tandem_core::access::resolve`; adapters map what they're given and no longer cap unattended runs themselves, so a routine's explicit Full grant (M13) isn't overridden. Unattended thread rows count as routine runs without a grant until routines and tasks pass their own.
- D-119: Threads perf suite details (UI-21, ARCHITECTURE §17). (1) The first thread opened in a session pays a one-time paint warm-up whatever its size (1.4 s on this software-GL VM, 0.8 s even for a 60-event thread), so the suite opens a small thread first and records that as `baseline.coldFirstOpenMs`; the targets then measure what a thread's size costs. The idle frame rate and a plain thread's worst open frame are recorded beside them, so a miss can be read against the machine. (2) MarkdownView's parse stays on the main thread (MILESTONES M7 task 7 says a worker): with the parse cache, mounting rows while scrolling costs a few ms of script per frame; what's left is layout and paint. (3) Rows mount and unmount a screenful at a time (the rendered range is kept while it covers the viewport by 400 px, then replaced by one covering it by 1,200 px), unchanged rows keep their objects while a turn streams, and an oversized message is line-aligned blocks in the transcript's own scroll: a nested scroller cost a 430 ms first paint here.
- D-120: ACP details F-TH-06 leaves open. (1) Models: an agent reports them on `session/new|load` (`models.availableModels`, `currentModelId`) and the thread's model is chosen with `session/set_model`; both are marked unstable in the protocol, so a refused `set_model` counts as a withdrawn model. Until an agent has reported models, its saved model is passed through instead of dropped by the catalog. The spec's "Automatic" is the catalog's first row, which UI_SPEC calls "Default". (2) ACP has no `delete`/`move` verbs on our rows: they show as edits (the card says Delete or Move). (3) Read access: ACP has no sandbox, so edit, delete and move permission requests are declined at Read (and Plan) without a card, and `fs/write_text_file` is refused; commands still ask. (4) "New credentials on the next turn": a sign-in error marks the session stale, so the next turn starts a new agent process with the account's current env (loading the old session when the agent can); an agent that reads its own config per request needs nothing more. (5) The gateway's tasks server (`tandem_tasks`, §6.8.3) joins with Tasks (M10). (6) Agents that only take stdio MCP servers get none; their approvals still come in-band.
- D-121: Plan → implement details F-TH-10 leaves open. (1) Implement keeps the composer's unsent text in the composer (UI_SPEC §4.3) rather than queueing it as a separate message (PRODUCT_SPEC, MILESTONES): text the user hasn't sent is never sent for them. (2) The message is PRODUCT_SPEC's "Implement plan version {n} exactly as written:" followed by the plan (UI_SPEC's "with the plan attached"). (3) A plan is the turn's last non-empty assistant reply, saved only when a Plan-mode turn completes (not stopped or failed). (4) "Revisions are listed in the thread inspector": until the inspector arrives with the changes panel, the header's "Plan versions (n)" button opens the list (UI_SPEC's ⋯ menu entry).
- D-122: Changes panel details F-TH-13/UI-24 leave open. (1) The start is recorded just before the thread's first turn runs, not at creation: a thread can be created long before it's used, and a folder can be added to it later. (2) The non-git manifest hashes with SHA-256 (ARCHITECTURE §7 said blake3, which isn't in the stack). (3) Shown now: the list, a row's diff, Refresh, Copy file list, the empty, loading, error and snapshot states, the narrow-window float, and the Files tab. Not yet: the "All uncommitted" scope, Commit…, Discard all…, Open diff in the main area, file-watch refresh (turn ends refresh it) and dragging the panel's width; they come with the git write actions (F-CD-20's commit flow).
- D-124: Engine switch summary limits F-TH-16 leaves open: each message is cut at 2,000 characters, the latest 40 completed tool actions within the kept messages are named (verb and argument summary, 120 characters), reasoning and failed or denied actions are left out, and the fence can't be closed from inside it. The summary is sent once: it stays pending until a turn's input reaches the new engine, so a failed start sends it again.
- D-125: A turn starts with its message (the reducer drops anything else at seq 0 as a straggler), so the switch divider is stored right after the message and the transcript draws it above. The seeded summary is the engine's input, never a transcript row.
- D-126: The browser demo (vibezeo.com/demo, user request after the second startup-program rejection). The desktop interface is built for the browser (`pnpm --filter desktop build:demo`, `vite.demo.config.ts` → `apps/web/public/demo/app/`, part of `build:site` on Vercel) against `src/demo/backend.ts`, a simulated core behind the existing `setInvokeBackend` seam: a seeded workspace with two agent panes and a shell, two threads, a scripted agent turn (read, edit, an approval before the tests, the result, Changes), a small line-editing shell, and quiet defaults for everything else. App boot now runs on a fake backend too (only the demo installs one before render). The demo bar and the /demo page say the agents are simulated and nothing leaves the browser; the marketing pages stay script-free (F-WB-01 AC2), the demo is a separate page with first-party scripts only and makes no network calls (CSP `connect-src 'self'`).
- D-123: Grid details F-TH-14 leaves open. (1) Mod+Alt+Arrow belongs to the `pane.focus_*` commands (bindings are unique in the registry), so those commands gained the `pane_or_cell` predicate and move between grid cells in Thread mode. (2) The plan's `threadGrid` decides how many cells show, but the saved grid isn't trimmed on a downgrade: it shows one thread on Free and comes back on Pro. (3) "Open side by side" in the rail row's menu is the keyboard path to dragging. (4) In a cell the Changes panel always floats (cells are narrow).
- D-127: The Free plan's chat engines (PRODUCT_SPEC §3.2 "Chat engines in Thread": Free runs Claude Code and Codex, Pro and Team all; UI_SPEC UI-11 Engine select). (1) The core enforces it from the entitlement's signed `chatEngines` (`Limits::allows_chat_engine`) where a thread is made, switched to another engine, sends (a queued follow-up too) and implements a plan, with `forbidden_plan` "Free threads run Claude Code and Codex. Pro runs every chat engine." Handoff to a Thread goes through the same create. (2) It applies to Thread mode only, as the matrix row says; teammate chats follow their own limits (M10 decides). (3) After a lapse, a thread made on Pro keeps its history and stays readable; it can't start a turn until it's switched to Claude Code or Codex (the composer shows an accent gate card with "See plans" and keeps the engine picker usable). (4) A new thread doesn't start on a remembered engine the plan no longer runs; it takes Claude Code, else the first allowed chat engine. (5) The engine picker disables the others with "Pro"; the handoff dialog's Thread target says "needs Pro". The early-access build (`TANDEM_HOSTED=0`) is Free, so it runs ACP engines in terminal panes only; the site and CHANGELOG say so. The plans dialog's gate line was the panes sentence for the grid gate too; each gate now has its own.
- D-128: The interactive demo on the home page (docs/FEATURES_TO_BUILD.md "The interactive demo on the website"). (1) Site rule: the home page loads exactly one first-party script, `/demo/embed.js` (no framework, no other origin, no storage), which frames `/demo/app/?embed=1` in a same-origin iframe; the F-WB-01 AC2 test now allows exactly that script on `/` and still forbids frames in the pages' HTML and any third-party resource. Vercel serves `/demo/app/*` with `frame-ancestors 'self'` and `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` (a rule after the global one, which keeps `DENY`/`'none'` everywhere else). (2) The static export still hydrates with Next's runtime, and hydration replaced the section the loader had changed (the frame vanished, found by the site E2E), so the frame's stage is inner HTML (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML`), which hydration leaves alone, and the loader touches nothing outside it. (3) The loader frames the demo when the section is within 200 px of the viewport, after the page's own `load` (or 2 s); under 1024 px it waits for "Tap to explore". Full screen is the same frame made `position: fixed` (never moved in the DOM, so nothing reloads); Esc closes it from the page, and from inside the demo through a same-origin `postMessage` when the app didn't use the key. (4) The demo runs the Free plan the 0.3.0 download runs (it said Pro with mixed limits before), so it never shows more than the download. (5) Limits live in the page's memory: 12 messages a visit and 500 characters a message; past that the core answers "Demo limit reached…" and the bar shows it with Download for Linux. Reset the demo reloads the page, which rebuilds the sample world. Browser storage holds only `vibezeo-demo-hint-dismissed`. (6) The sample world: three workspaces (shop, api, docs-site), Claude Code, Codex and Gemini CLI panes plus a shell (the Free plan's four), the Dashboard with Working, Waiting for you and Done, a finished thread, a thread switched from Claude Code to Codex with its divider, the scripted fix, Plan-mode turns that make plan versions (Implement works), `git diff` in the shell, and the Files tab, git panel and Scratchpad as before.
- D-129: 0.3.0 includes the Scratchpad (F-SH-12, merged into `product-m0-m5` before the release build) and D-804's release-build shortcut fix. The CHANGELOG lists only what the build does; it has no "Fixed" list, because neither the plans dialog's grid-gate line nor the release build's Ctrl+S were ever in a published build (0.2.0 was never deployed).
- D-130: The desktop E2E runner gives the app a zsh login shell (`SHELL`), whatever shell runs the suite. The specs were written for zsh (E2E-M4-1 writes `~/.zshrc`), and some containers put a real `claude` on bash's login PATH through `/etc/profile.d`, which made E2E-M4-1 find it as a shell install instead of the nvm folder under test.

## Log
- M0 (earlier session): first slices of core, engine registry, local store, desktop shell frame, API health, CI.
- M0: build kit and plan committed (`build-kit/`, `docs/PLAN.md`).
- M0: realigned to DECISIONS: crate rename to `tandem-engines`, seven new crates, Tauri crate with capabilities and CSP, ts-rs protocol generation, UI_SPEC tokens, ICU i18n, rewritten core (redactor, containment, settings schema, keychain, logging, store with writer thread and full schema), API restructured to ARCHITECTURE §14.1, web on the shared tokens, dev/gate/coverage/trace/CI scripts, desktop E2E harness. Both M0 E2E scenarios pass. M0 closed.
- M1: backend accounts, entitlements, remote config, mock billing, edge limits, desktop auth server side, account portal pages. 37 API tests, 6 Playwright scenarios, Rust verifiers with an interop fixture. M1 closed.
- M2: desktop shell, design system and sign-in. `tandem-pty` (reader/batcher/flow control/ring/query responder/tree kill/login env), core `net` + `telemetry`, store repos for workspaces/tabs/panes, desktop services (api, auth, entitlement, deeplink, workspaces, terminal, telemetry, events), the M2 command set and plugins, `@tandem/ui` kit with gallery, and the desktop screens. Fixed on the way: boot waited on an animation frame a hidden window never gets; channel reuse lost PTY output; Settings Esc could miss a non-element target; the terminal stole focus back from Settings' opener. M0's boot spec now asserts the first real screen (the splash is transient since M2). All M2 E2E scenarios pass. M2 closed.
- M3 (in progress): pane grid, picker and drag and drop; command registry, dispatcher and palette; rail, tab strip and missing-folder flow; `tandem-pty` side channel and child registry. Fixed on the way: a new pane took focus twice and could steal it back from Settings' opener; Escape in an inline rename still committed through the blur; the rail tree owned list markup (axe); `gate.sh` could pass with a failed step.
- M3 (cont.): handback and Saved sessions, engine registry slice, Windows Job Object, WebGL pool and rendering options, clipboard/paste/drops/keys, find bar, per-run orphan registry, E2E-M3-4…8, 12, 14…16 specs. Fixed on the way: a quick relaunch reported the old instance's dying children as orphans and could overwrite the new run's registry; the E2E reset left the UI showing the previous spec's closed pane; zombies were counted as orphans; a closed pane could still be spawned.
- M3 closed: output scheduler, batched WebGL pool and staggered mounts (mode switch 1,947 → ~150 ms, six agents framed 4,176 → 670 ms here), perf suite on a `--profile perf` build, UTF-8 pane locale; full desktop suite green; tags `m3a`/`m3`.
- M4: registry with remote overrides, detection, provider homes and accounts, launch specs, HMAC status bus with `tandem-hook` and heuristics, live status in panes and the rail, launch grid, install sheet, Settings › Engines, title chain, readiness and `pty_type_prompt`, auto-arrange, Card view, collapsed sessions; `fake-engine`; E2E-M4-1…7. Fixed on the way: a status overflow panic on the first output of a pane, the install sheet dying with the empty tab's grid, auto-arrange widths off by half a gutter, the E2E app inheriting the runner's environment. Restored this Log section and ARCHITECTURE's tail after a scripted edit cut them short.
- M5: approval broker, right sidebar, pane names and header, Dashboard with a fixture source, notification center, OS notifications with a recording notifier, Settings › Notifications; session restore with paused cards, a two-slot restore queue, launch configurations, auto-resume, bounded parallel quit, splash phases and kill-fuzz; the updater with a signed mock feed, pill, restart dialog, Settings › Updates and the minimum-version notice; the release workflow, local dry run, notices and SBOMs; the onboarding framework; the startup perf suite; E2E-M5-1…9. Fixed on the way: an approval opened from a sync command crashed the app (no runtime), notification paging skipped same-millisecond items, the E2E-M2-2 axe check read a half-faded sheet, a SessionStart hook could beat its session row, and the child registry could lose a child to two simultaneous spawns. M5 closed.
- Site (M5 pause, F-WB-01 slice): static public site for vibezeo.com (home, about, privacy, terms, security, 404, robots, sitemap, security.txt), `build:site`, site tests; checked under the production CSP at desktop and phone widths. Moved from Cloudflare Pages to Vercel at the owner's request: `vercel.json` replaces `_headers`, verified with `vercel build` on a clean copy, and its skip rule tested against first-deploy, desktop-only, web and unknown-SHA cases.
- Site on `main`: so Vercel deploys from `main`, the site went there on its own (`5943c8c`: the site pages, the tokens, fonts, Badge and StatusDot from `@tandem/ui`, `vercel.json`). The rest of M0–M5 reaches `main` through a pull request from `product-m0-m5`, which merges `main` back. `apps/site` (an earlier one-page draft from another session) is kept but not deployed.
- Public site for the Claude startup program: a Built with Claude section (Claude Code hooks and `--resume`, built with Claude Code, hosted AI features planned on the Claude API), real app screenshots (`apps/desktop/e2e/screens/site.screens.ts`), real progress figures and a changelog, a Request early access email, founder and company facts. Removed a claim the app doesn't support yet: approving tool requests from Dashboard cards (no real approval source until Thread mode). Then, after looking at a selected applicant's site (onemana.dev): `/pricing/` with the launch plans from PRODUCT_SPEC §3.1 (marked "Pricing at launch", planned features badged), and an unedited screen recording of the real app (45 s since the re-capture) (`apps/desktop/e2e/screens/demo.screens.ts`: Claude Code detected, four terminals, kill -9, restore), served as VP9 WebM and H.264 MP4.
- Linux release and rename (site on `main`, merged back here): vibezeo.com names the product Vibezeo, says 0.1.0 is released for Linux and serves the .deb and SHA256SUMS from `apps/web/public/downloads/`, links no code repository, shows no years, and drops wording that read as unfinished (site tests pin all of it). The desktop app's visible name is Vibezeo too (window title, catalog, sign-in page, diagnostics); internal names (`~/.tandem`, crates, bundle id) stay. Screenshots and the 45 s demo were re-captured from the renamed app.
- Credibility pass for the startup-program review: the founder's name links to their X profile; same-day features on every platform is stated as a goal; the progress strip counts the code 0.1.0 was built from (862 automated tests, 42 E2E suites); the welcome screen, account menu, plans sheet, Account & Plan and the sign-in refusal say "Free plan" and "Pro and Team start with Vibezeo accounts" instead of "early access" and "at launch". This branch then merges into `main` (pull request #2, `97b7ae6`), so `main` is the code that shipped, and the .deb on vibezeo.com is rebuilt from it (welcome screen: "Free plan").
- Site: the home page's video is now a 1-minute product video (`public/screens/product.{webm,mp4}`, poster `product-poster.jpg`) in place of the 45 s unedited recording, at the owner's request. Its app shots are one screen recording of the released 0.1.0 build on a 2x display (driven with xdotool), with motion graphics, an original synthesized soundtrack and sound effects (Remotion); the terminal part is marked 2.3× speed and the live-status scene is labelled an illustration, and the caption says both. htop's command column was hidden for the take so the internal binary name doesn't show.
- Fix (M5 notifications, found by the M6 E2E regression): an agent's "finished" notification was skipped when its "working" and "done" arrived in the same 50 ms status batch (a quick turn), since the service saw done → done. It now also compares when the status began (`since`), so a done that began after the last one seen notifies; `notification_for` is unit-tested, and E2E-M5-5 passes again. E2E-M6-4 reads the conflict list with `textContent` (WebKit's `getText` is empty while a dialog fades in).
- Fix (M5 status, found by the M6 E2E regression): a hooked agent's first report is SessionStart, which switched the output heuristics off without giving the agent a status. Depending on which arrived first, a just-started agent had no status (missing from the Dashboard and from the update dialog's live agents: E2E-M5-7 failed 2 of 3 runs) or stayed "working" from its startup banner. The first SessionStart now sets Idle (`f_en_05_a_started_hooked_agent_is_idle_whichever_arrives_first`); E2E-M5-7 then passed 3 of 3.
- M6 usage and context (F-AC-04, F-AC-06): the title-bar usage chip reads Codex's windows through `codex app-server` and says "Usage unavailable" with the reason for engines that don't report usage; agent pane headers show the context meter from the engine's own transcript at each Stop. E2E-M6-8 passes 3 of 3. Its screenshots found three layout faults, now fixed: the chip wrapped, the omnibox label wrapped, and with the meters the title bar clipped the window controls at 1440 px (D-113).
- M6 closed: the onboarding Accounts step (D-114) completes the exit checklist, and the full E2E regression passes 48 of 48 suites. The first full run failed E2E-M5-9 only: earlier suites had already created Claude's default account, so "Sign in to Claude Code" opened Sign in again instead of Add account. A fresh test profile now also removes engine accounts, as a real first run has none (reproduced with m5-8 then m5-9 failing; passes after the fix).
- M7 task 1 (F-TH-03): normalized thread events, the chat adapter traits, the engine process that every adapter will own (Stop is over within 3.5 s even for an engine that ignores stdin and SIGINT: 3.40 s, five runs), thread storage, and the turn service with its eight IPC commands, plus the webview's reducer. No engine adapter yet, so no UI; the Claude adapter is task 3. While building, the disk filled with 9.8 GB of stale `tandem*` build artifacts in `target/debug/deps`; deleting them freed it.
- M7 tasks 2–3 (F-PL-01 slice, F-TH-04): the loopback MCP gateway with leases and the approvals tool, and the Claude Code adapter (flags, stream-json parser with reviewed fixture goldens, session with interrupt and exit reports). A Claude thread now runs end to end in the core: the launcher starts the gateway, leases a session, writes its MCP config, and Claude's permission requests become approval cards. The disk filled twice from build caches; builds in this session now run with `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`.
- M7 tasks 4–5 and task 6's core (F-TH-05, F-TH-09, F-TH-11): the Codex adapter over JSON-RPC with in-band approvals, the version gate and resume after an idle exit; access resolved in one place (Plan reads only, unattended never Full without a grant); approvals expire into a deny at 30 minutes; "Allow for this thread" rules per §6.10. Codex's protocol names couldn't be checked against a real install here (HUMAN_TODO).
- M7 task 9 core and task 8 backend (F-TH-08, F-TH-01, F-TH-02, F-TH-07, F-TH-15): the model catalog, the remaining thread commands (update, archive, delete with a recovery copy, move to folder, switch engine, drafts, attachments, `@` search, `models_list`), unread, local titles with AI titles behind a Privacy opt-in (D-118), and the silent-turn note. A test caught the `@` search escaping the folder through `../` (a lexical `starts_with` check); it now refuses `..` and absolute parts and compares canonical paths.
- M7 tasks 6–9 UI (F-TH-01, F-TH-02, F-TH-07, F-TH-11, F-TH-12): the Thread mode screen replaces the interim one: rail, header, virtualized transcript, the composer wired to drafts, attachments, mentions and the toolbar, and inline approval cards. Every surface stays mounted, so the composer takes focus only while Thread mode shows (it would otherwise pull focus from Code mode's terminal at launch).
- E2E-M7-1…4 (F-TH-02, F-TH-03, F-TH-07, F-TH-11, F-TH-15, D-6): 13 cases pass. They found two product bugs, both fixed: a turn that ended before the webview subscribed never showed (the view now subscribes first, then reads history and replays live events on top, with a unit test), and a pasted screenshot did nothing on Linux (WebKitGTK doesn't list a copied image as a file item; a paste with an image type or no text now asks the core to read the clipboard). Threads now also appear on the Dashboard.
- E2E-M7-5 (F-TH-05): a killed idle app-server restarts on the next send with "Reconnecting…", resumes its thread and keeps the history; Codex 0.31.0 fails closed with the version message. It found that `engines_list` still reported every engine as not chat-capable (a placeholder from before the adapters), so once the engine list loaded Thread mode said it needed a chat engine; `chat_capable` now follows the adapters Thread mode has (Claude Code, Codex).
- Threads perf suite (UI-21): a 5,000-event thread opens in 221 ms and pages back in 43 ms on the perf build. Profiling in the page found four costs, all fixed: the list re-rendered on frames where its visible rows hadn't changed and mounted rows one per frame while scrolling (now a screenful at a time: 15.5 → 34.8 fps), rows re-rendered on every streamed delta (new row objects each time; unchanged rows now keep theirs), messages re-parsed whenever they scrolled back into view (a parse cache), and a 2 MB reply's preview sat in a nested scroller whose first paint took 430 ms (now line-aligned blocks in the transcript, revealed a block per frame: 384 → 165 ms). Scroll fps and the fence's worst frame stay [manual]; this VM's software GL is the floor (D-119).
- M7 task 10 (F-TH-06): the ACP adapter for Cursor Agent, Grok and OpenCode, with golden fixtures for the protocol and for whole sessions against scripted agents, the agent's models in the picker with the withdrawn-model fallback, sign-in errors with Sign in, and E2E-M7-12 (4 cases) against a fake ACP agent. Running every M7 spec together found that an earlier spec's remembered engine leaked into later specs (they run in name order, `m7-12` before `m7-2`); the E2E reset now clears the thread defaults.
- M7 task 11 (F-TH-10): plan versions from Plan-mode turns, the Plan card with Implement / Revise / Copy, "Plan versions (n)" in the header, and `thread_implement_plan`; E2E-M7-6 passes, and all seven M7 specs pass together (20 cases).
- M7 task 12 (F-TH-13): the Changes and Files panel with its start point per thread, `thread_changes` and `thread_file_read`; E2E-M7-9 passes and all eight M7 specs pass together (22 cases). A unit test caught the Files list forgetting that a file was edited when it was read again later.
- M7 task 13 (F-TH-14): the thread grid with drag from the rail, the plan limit, the saved layout and cell focus; Thread mode's view became a per-thread `ThreadView` that a cell or the single view shows. E2E-M7-7 passes (relaunching signs in again: the E2E keychain lives in memory), and all nine M7 specs pass together (25 cases). A unit test caught the Free limit losing to "Grid is full" when a six-cell grid was saved on Pro.
- M7 task 14 (F-TH-16): engine switch with the summary seed, the divider and per-engine usage; "Interrupted — Resume?"; handoff to a Thread. E2E-M7-8, 10 and 11 pass. E2E-M7-8 found that a divider stored before the message hid the whole turn live (the reducer starts turns from the message), so it now follows the message (D-125); its screenshot also showed short user messages wrapping a letter or two per line (the bubble's 72% cap measured against a shrink-to-fit wrapper), fixed by capping the wrapper.
- Browser demo (D-126): vibezeo.com/demo explains it and opens /demo/app/, the real interface on a simulated core; walked through in Chromium (send, approve, result, Changes, Code mode, shell) with no console errors.
- M7 regression: 59/60 spec files passed on the first run; E2E-M3-9 F-SH-09.6 ran every registry command from the palette in Code mode, where `thread.changes` (enabled only with a thread open) can't run. The spec now opens a thread first for `thread_open` commands; 60/60 pass. Tag `m7` (local).
- 0.2.0 (M6 + M7): version bump, CHANGELOG section, and the site's release, changelog (M6, M7), features, roadmap and progress figures (8 milestones, 1,235 automated tests, 60 E2E spec files).
- Handover: `docs/OPERATING.md` (setup, the work loop, commands, E2E lessons, release, site deploy and checks, monitoring, non-negotiables), `scripts/agent/` (infra wrapper, target pruning, offline AppImage smoke test, demo walkthrough) and the product video's sources in `media/product-video/`, so another session or person can run the whole project from the repo. `CLAUDE.md` points to the handbook.
- Free plan chat engines (F-TH-06, D-127, release-0.3.0): Free threads run Claude Code and Codex only, enforced in the core from the signed limits (create, switch, send, implement), with the engine picker's "Pro" rows, the composer's gate card for a lapsed ACP thread, the handoff dialog and the plans dialog's own gate line; the site's plan copy says it (pricing, home, download). E2E-M7-12 now runs on a Pro account and adds the Free case. The tests found that the plans dialog showed the panes sentence for the grid gate.
- E2E environment (D-130): this container's bash login shell put a real `claude` on PATH (`/etc/profile.d/nodejs.sh`), and its `$SHELL` is bash, so E2E-M4-1 failed (claude found by the shell, not the nvm folder; no slow-shell diagnostic from `~/.zshrc`). The runner now gives the app zsh; 65/65 spec files passed with it. Docker Hub answered pulls with 429 and `with-infra.sh` hid it (sign-up 500s); the images come from `mirror.gcr.io` now and the script says when `infra:up` fails (OPERATING §3). `release-local.sh` also needs `cargo-about --features cli` and `cargo-cyclonedx`.
- Interactive demo on the home page (D-128): `/demo/embed.js`, the home page's frame with its still image (`public/demo/still.jpg`, a capture of the demo's first screen), Explore full screen and Tap to explore, the `/demo/app/*` frame headers, and in the demo the first-steps hint, the 12-message and 500-character limits, Reset, the Free plan, three workspaces with a Gemini CLI pane, the Dashboard's three states, a switched thread, Plan-mode turns and `git diff`. 15 demo unit tests and 5 browser tests (`e2e-site/demo-embed.spec.ts`, acceptance 1–6). The browser tests found two bugs: Next's hydration replaced the section the loader had changed (the frame vanished), so the stage is now inner HTML; and on phones the Tap to explore overlay stayed over the open demo.
- Two bugs found while preparing 0.3.0, both fixed with tests: (1) the layout store's `load()` copied every tab before its awaits and wrote the copy back, so workspaces loading at the same time wiped each other's panes and the app then opened a stray shell in the emptied tab (the demo's three workspaces showed it; `stores/layout.test.ts`); it now merges only its own workspace's tabs into the latest state. (2) E2E-M2-1 assumed it ran right after E2E-M0-1, but `m16-5-scratchpad` sorts before it and leaves an offline profile, so the full regression failed it; it now starts from `freshSignedOut()`.
- Release 0.3.0: version bump, CHANGELOG, the .deb in `apps/web/public/downloads` with SHA256SUMS, release.ts, progress figures, home features (Files, git and a browser; Scratchpad) and roadmap. Gate green; full E2E regression on the final tree (see the pull request).
